Up/Down, Home/End and parent/child Left/Right focus movement over registered rows.
Nearest tree/table ownership, collapsed/disabled ancestors and modal admission
constrain targets; selection and loading remain app-owned. The new public-event
regression checks hierarchy, repeat/consumed input, same-batch collapse/movement,
unloaded children, table paths and modal restoration. Previous static/shared
SDKs reject the first navigation step. An initial ancestor-eligibility defect
was separately reproduced and corrected before final acceptance; its source and
failed probe remain retained. Final strict installed static/shared/sanitizer
consumers pass old-tree and navigation checks. Regular suites pass 145/151,
144/150 and 145/151, retaining six display skips each. See the
[navigation report](tree-navigation-report.md) for exact scope and artifacts.
This advances L06/I01/Q02; async hierarchy UI, virtual focus reveal, breadcrumbs,
richer tree-table relationships, real pickers/services and native/human adoption
remain open. Public API/ABI, SDK 0.1.23, Aetheri sources and its pin are unchanged.

## Tree expansion retains the original key target (2026-11-07)

Source `bae657a4cdaaed6e4f6dc426b71ad17c69586c12` corrects tree expansion after
same-frame focus changes. Previously, Right on one branch followed by focus
movement could expand another branch. The existing per-event routing metadata
now preserves the original row/expander target. Nine added public-event cases
check model and semantic expansion, focus and selection. Three previous installed
SDK artifacts detect the original mismatch; three new strict C17 installed
static/shared/sanitizer consumers pass. Regular root suites pass 144/150,
143/149 and 144/150 respectively, retaining six external-display skips each.
Initial negative-test cleanup diagnostics and the cleanup-safe final negative
are preserved separately. See the [tree routing report](tree-input-routing-report.md)
for exact artifacts and scope. This advances L06/I01/Q02; asynchronous hierarchy
loading, full navigation, real pickers/services and native/human acceptance remain
open. Aetheri, its pin, SDK API/ABI and version 0.1.23 are unchanged.

## Original Files document save and close lifetime (2026-11-07)

Consumer implementation [1f9780edc7efba0878edea5d8ea153613506386b](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/1f9780edc7efba0878edea5d8ea153613506386b)
extends the existing original-Files build with an optional original document
path. Aetheri remains frozen at `27de5cb228da0f37264fad64d964ad7a660d210d`.
The original Files controller/launch code, Terminal, editor/document/file code
and save/close policy are reused without application-source changes. The new
`NEXAUI_OWNED_FILES_DOCUMENTS` option defaults OFF. ON additionally verifies 20
Terminal input hashes beside the original 38 inputs and builds the original SDL
Terminal and separate toolkit-free C17 editor. No SDL Files view is built.

This is an external application integration profile. The selected original
Terminal requires its reviewed local SDL3 3.4.20, SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 and Wayland-client
1.26.0 packages. They do not enter NexaUI or the Files host. OFF neither searches
for SDL nor builds the document programs. Exact source/archive selection and
explicit runtime-library paths are recorded; there is no download, global setup,
normal consumer-pin update or modification of Aetheri's ongoing work.

The new `--document` native mode first runs all six original independent Files
peer modes, then moves focus to the selected literal `seed.txt`, localizes Files
and opens its original held-FD document path. It verifies exact original Terminal
and editor executables/hashes/parents while holding pidfds and retaining process
maps. The original Terminal reports Wayland/opengles2 readiness and admits native
keyboard input into the original editor. Neither a copied SDK editor buffer nor
a shell command substitutes for that application model.

Native input creates the unsaved `helloseed` draft. Closing the Terminal window
retains the original dirty-content decision and leaves the file's original
`seed` bytes untouched. Escape cancels the choice. An independent AT-SPI action
then requests the localized Files Close while the document owns keyboard focus.
Files publishes its waiting state and retains both original actors. Explicit
Ctrl+S saves exactly `helloseed` (SHA-256
`e12c017eaada16e2355e85d52e366a99b6ef5b871e2a4ef15a33861cdfbc276e`),
without changing either actor or launching another document. Only explicit
document close ends the original pair; Files observes `DOCUMENT_END status=0`
and exits normally. Final screenshots show the dirty editor choice and Files'
German waiting message. Those are selected virtual-output observations, not a
full visual or physical three-monitor acceptance result.

All three accessible native profiles pass: reused static Release, reused static
Debug ASan/UBSan, and a newly built installed shared Release SDK with AT-SPI.
Each runs seven original independent peer invocations including localized Close,
for 21 successful invocations in total. Files exits 0, both original actor pidfds
report end, and owned helpers terminate without forced-kill escalation. The editor
process maps contain neither SDL nor NexaUI; the original Terminal maps SDL.
Shared runtime dependencies themselves are not sanitizer-instrumented merely
because the original application C17 sources and static SDK are instrumented.
Leak detection and halt-on-error remain enabled without suppressions.

Four final core profiles each pass all three original CTests without skips:
static Release, static ASan/UBSan, accessible shared Release, and provider-off
shared Release with documents OFF. The latter configures/builds without an SDL
prefix, renders, fails native discovery at the intended boundary and closes
normally by keyboard. Original controller/view assertions and 10/30-second
limits remain unchanged. Strict C17 conversion/shadow warnings and Werror remain
enabled for original application sources. Native campaigns retain the existing
90-second outer limit and original independent peer assertions/deadlines.

An isolated early-close mutant sets Files' running flag false when Close is
requested with the original document still open. The same native document test
detects it after actor verification and the dirty-close capture; the mutant owner
exits 125. This is not counted as a positive case. Normal original source and
library hashes remain unchanged. Failure cleanup uses only the retained private
actor pidfds; it does not target a live desktop or numeric unrelated PIDs.

The accessible shared SDK was built from exact NexaUI
`5118d3afd048a94430ca649fb345fb51f4f6965f`, with X11/Vulkan/examples/root tests off,
AT-SPI on and its private reviewed D-Bus headers. The cache confirms compiled
D-Bus support. Library SHA-256 is
`20860b2bb8f4407d718d416da5b0d605372f4c172fae30dff54d0de589246559`.
SDK production source/API/ABI/version 0.1.23 are unchanged by this consumer step.
The independent [base CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42314667944)
finished with all seven required jobs successful. These external consumer cases
are separate from root CI and are not automatically executed there.

An additional SDK-wide global-Werror build failed on existing initializer,
indentation and line-break maybe-uninitialized warnings. Its source, flags and
failure logs are retained. The successful shared SDK uses the project's original
standard flags, preserving those diagnostics; no warning is disabled and no
warning-free SDK claim is made. Existing static SDK artifacts are reused as
before. The warning-clean core build remains an explicit open engineering item.

Evidence is `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-owned-documents-20261107.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`c2783a7bb33573b4a9cd3e57d8721ba9367305b8a84f35287fbb11c693be7110`.
All 1,652 manifest files and four symbolic links are independently checked after
local transfer. The archive retains exact consumer/source archives, all selected
builds and flags, installed shared SDK, source-input proofs, logs/JUnit, normal
and negative native campaigns, full raw PPMs and runtime path/inode/hash records.
Runtime files are hashed at collection, not claimed as in-memory code hashes.
Trees are streamed without duplicating gigabytes of raw captures on the guest.
Guest/host clock differences and overwritten intermediate helper-byte limitations
remain explicit. No frozen prior evidence is removed or rewritten.

This advances B02/B07/D07/P05/Q04 and supersedes the earlier document-path gap only
for this explicit ordinary-user selected-document/save/close flow. Normal
development-owner launch, real document grants, complete Files/Terminal suites,
managed holder/lease drain, uncertain-save recovery, installed IMEs, full native
chrome/scale/loss and human/physical acceptance remain open. The child uses the
original SDL Terminal presentation; this does not claim a NexaUI Terminal view.
Actual authorities, picker workflows, device/software integrations, the known
unpaced Files sanitizer burst and held-pointer loss issue are still open. No
1.0.0 release or Aetheri pin change is implied.

## Original Aetheri-owned Files on the candidate SDK (2026-11-07)

Consumer implementation [973f228efd07dc55282502eb7623f001e196c9cd](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/973f228efd07dc55282502eb7623f001e196c9cd)
adds an external build of Aetheri's original Files composition and controller,
selected from immutable Aetheri `27de5cb228da0f37264fad64d964ad7a660d210d`.
Its original list/grid, held-folder sidebar, filter, naming/rename/move dialogs,
information and worker/unknown-state models are reused; no competing model or
application UI is introduced. Thirty-eight original inputs are hashed at
configure and before native execution. A changed original input also forces
reconfiguration: an isolated post-configure edit is rejected before compilation.
The selected original Git archive hashes to
`ab6f36cf98b45fb36f006173e7432a6e676f2ed53003e3747d2839d33421e812`.

This standalone project deliberately selects an installed SDK >=0.1.23. It does
not execute, alter or claim satisfaction of Aetheri's own exact-0.1.18 package

Aetheri's working changes, source, package selection and runtime remain untouched.

On Fedora 44/GCC 16.2.1, static Release AT-SPI on, shared Release AT-SPI off and

Unicode editing, whole-commit rejection, modal bounds and the minimum-size hint.
The original 10/30-second limits and strict C17 warnings, including conversion/

and halt-on-error. These are application-view checks, not timing/memory budgets.

The unchanged original `test_files_view_fault.py` passed all three profiles on
the pinned private Aetheri compositor: nine create/rename/move cases in total.
Its separate fault host performs actual ordinary-user effects in new private
folders, holds the worker acknowledgement and then injects the original error.
The script checks one syscall attempt, retained inode/content, truthful unknown
state, blocked new mutation and original close/wait behavior. No document child
starts. The supplied existing Terminal/editor binaries are not exercised by these
cases. The normal host contains none of the fault hooks. These are not managed

The original independent libatspi client then passed six modes against the normal

the expected client boundary and still closes normally by keyboard. The private
compositor/bus/registry all terminate without forced-kill escalation. Process
maps contain no SDL, X11, EGL or Vulkan presentation libraries in these hosts.

Final native Grid/selection and rejected-draft images were inspected. The single

for the emoji; exact Unicode remains readable to the independent Text client.
Font fallback, final styling and human reading acceptance remain open. Native
three-output captures are transport/geometry evidence on virtual outputs, not a

files match source `4eed8f0373ba33e490cb4307339e1b8fc338feee` and current base

## Revisioned removal snapshots and native view rebinding (2026-11-07)

Core implementation [784c484ca8fc7f1f3c73e5b4fcee97cdb8a1758f](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/784c484ca8fc7f1f3c73e5b4fcee97cdb8a1758f)
and native consumer/tests [0c4587d52c9e7deba6da85099651941a19489e4d](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/0c4587d52c9e7deba6da85099651941a19489e4d)
build on `38430c8c351459e2b2bb428296314418f7d7a27f`. The example owner
now accepts a copied strictly newer revision of the same plan, installation and
scope. Duplicate IDs and conflicting semantic/object mappings in overlapping
consecutive snapshots are rejected. Same/older revisions, changed scope and
revision wraparound do not replace state. This is an example-owned contract;
it does not assert that Aetheri exposes such a revision protocol.

Existing views retain their original immutable snapshot. They keep showing its
exact revision, disable confirmation and omit the old request action, exposing
Refresh. Rebinding stages a complete view and fresh context before retiring the
old publication. Allocation or validation failure preserves the old state.
Queued input admitted against the old context cannot confirm/request the newer
plan. Native controls/Text references retire rather than changing targets.

The replacement retains the applied name filter, the same query-field object,
draft/selection/undo, effective theme values, density and reduced motion. Active
preedit is cancelled. Closed-dialog focus moves to Filter; an open dialog keeps
its safe current control. The first visible object anchors by its full object
identity and intra-row offset across reordering and eager/virtual transitions;
removing that anchor goes Home. The bound window is explicitly a whole-review
window and must outlive all bound contexts. A successful rebind requires a new
frame/publication before native queries. No second view may publish into that
window while the binding is active.

A previously emitted request remains issued with the original exact correlation.
Even when preview revisions advance from 42 to 43 and 44, visible status and
standard native Description continue to identify request revision 42 and unknown
outcome. Refresh, reopen and reaching `UINT64_MAX` never rearm the request. There
is no backend dispatch, inferred completion or implicit retry.

Fedora 44/GCC 16.2.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Debug
each passed all five standalone CTests without skips. The new regression covers
three concurrent views, immutable older records, admitted-but-unexecuted actions,
live editor retention and rejected queued edits, exact original request identity,
empty/re-added snapshots, revision limits, 100,000-object reverse ordering,
viewport anchoring/removal, eager/virtual transitions and hidden unknown objects.
Allocation checks reject 39 injected failures in each static profile and 18 in
the shared executable, retaining caller data, owner/view state and balanced live
allocations. Executable wrapping does not inject allocations inside the DSO.
The original 30/15-second limits, leak detection and halt-on-error remain intact.

Fourteen native positive cases passed on the unchanged pinned Aetheri compositor,
seven each in Release and ASan/UBSan. They include explicit refresh of small and
filtered large previews, automatic refresh of an open large-view filter, two
refreshes after an issued request, and unfiltered/IME/incomplete regressions.
The independent libatspi client has no NexaUI linkage. It reads the old revision
before refresh and the new one afterwards, rejects retained old references,
checks draft/selection/focus and Ctrl+Z, observes controlled seven-byte preedit
then cancellation, and verifies the changed last object's full window-relative
visibility and exact ID/size. All positive hosts exit normally. A provider-off
negative maps but fails discovery as intended. Final small/large captures were
inspected. Issued-case images precede the request; subsequent revisions and
original-request status are proven through native Text/Description, not those
earlier captures. Driver timeouts and required original keyboard commands remain
unchanged, with two additional controlled editor commands for draft refresh.

Sixty complete existing small/filter PPM pairs are byte-identical across the three
profiles. Baseline source is checked against the base Git archive. Two isolated
mutants are detected: rearming `issued` fails the retained-request assertion;
keeping the old context fails the queued-toggle/request assertion on the new
view. Normal source and library hashes remain unchanged. Initial strict compiler
failures from misleading indentation are retained and were fixed without
suppressing warnings. Neither final native campaign had a failing case.

Snapshots retain only the current owner revision and those borrowed by live views;
there is no unbounded historical identity ledger. On the tested x86-64 build,
100,000 records occupy 45,600,000 copied bytes, group order 800,000 bytes and the
two lookup-pointer arrays 1,600,000 bytes per retained snapshot. These are
source-sized allocations, excluding view/font/framework and process RSS. No
memory/performance budget or SDL comparison is established.

Evidence is `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-removal-refresh-20261107.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`3441d8f9cfaf064010964c30cb47804e2cd4baf961ecc4fa1f693843b69e2bf7`.
All 942 manifest entries are independently checked after local transfer. Thirteen
compiled source/consumer/runner files match the final code revision after CRLF/LF
normalization; flags, binaries, SDK provenance, JUnit, both native campaigns,
complete before/after images, negative sources and intermediate source archives
are retained. Overwritten earlier intermediate binaries are not claimed.
The compositor SHA remains
`631ec1740d9de6daac790d2c29351df7ccb2cabce936ac6ab35ba59b8cb71c75`.
The guest clock is earlier than the host collection date; only copied source
mtimes were normalized to avoid build warnings, without changing system clocks.

SDK production sources, public API/ABI, dependencies and development version
0.1.23 are unchanged. Aetheri source/models and its documented 0.1.18 pin remain
independent. Actual `PrepareChange(remove)` transport/authority, real inventory,
service reconciliation, retention editing, durable recovery, installed IMEs,
locales, other platforms and physical/human acceptance remain open. The unpaced
Files sanitizer burst and held-pointer capability-loss issues are unchanged.
This advances L05/P02/P04/P07/A06 without closing those entire IDs or the catalogue.
Standalone examples are checked separately from root SDK CI. The [base CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42304729159)
finished with six successful platform jobs, while its sanitizer job was cancelled
during dependency installation; it is not a complete seven-job success.

## Full-plan-safe removal name filtering (2026-11-06)

Implementation [950af924d7aede2533d802fd21753c5073d88beb](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/950af924d7aede2533d802fd21753c5073d88beb)
adds a complete name-filter workflow to the existing removal composition, on base
`6a139f3c1a2b582216d9fdcdfc8b257494d95422`. It reuses `nx_search_filter`, described
text fields, modal/focus, scrolling and read-only text snapshots. The current
catalogue's field-description/style concern already has public described/styled
entry points in this SDK; no duplicate widget or SDK API is added here.

The modal separates draft and applied query. Enter/Apply commits; Cancel/Escape
discards the draft. Empty shows all. Applied input is bounded at 128 UTF-8 bytes
without NUL/newlines; invalid drafts disable Apply. Matching follows the existing
ASCII case-fold/exact-other-UTF-8 contract and searches names only. Matching and
index replacement happen on Apply, not on every edit. A changed filter clears
the complete-plan confirmation and resets scroll. A repeated identical query is
a no-op. Existing object IDs remain stable, while captions expose both shown and
complete category counts. Eligibility always uses the complete immutable owner
snapshot: an unknown object hidden by the query still blocks requests. A request
retains the original plan, installation, scope and revision and is never rearmed
by clearing the filter or replacing the view. This is presentation, not retention
editing or an authorization/operation API.

The host binds its existing window for IME/clipboard and routes events through
the example's queue wrapper. Escape is resolved after earlier queued activations,
so Enter+Escape in one batch cancels the opening dialog rather than closing the
app. The modal blocks background actions and restores focus. The active filter
is visibly labelled; header width is reserved before title layout. Window/context
borrowing rules are explicit. SDK production source, API/ABI, development version
0.1.23, dependencies and Aetheri source/models/pin remain unchanged.

Fedora 44/GCC 16.2.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Debug
each passed all four standalone CTests without skips. The new test covers 100,000
objects, exact last identity, empty results, invalid input, Unicode name matching,
draft cancellation, native edit admission, modal blocking/focus, full-scope
requests, hidden unknowns and same-batch Escape. Eight modal captures cover
dark/light, normal/doubled text and 640x480/420x320; the small eager path also
repeatedly opens/cancels. Constructor/filter allocation failures preserve the
previous applied state, owner intent and live allocation balance: 21 injected
failures in each static profile and eight in the shared executable. Executable
wrapping does not inject allocations inside the DSO. Existing 30/15-second test
limits, leak detection and sanitizer halt-on-error remain unchanged.

Twelve native positive cases passed on the pinned Aetheri compositor, six each
in Release and ASan/UBSan: large filter actions, large filter keys/capture,
hidden-unknown gating, small filter, controlled IME/keys/capture and the unfiltered
large regression. The independent libatspi client rejects late edits through old
and closed dialog fields and verifies that drafts do not change applied counts.
It scrolls until the filtered object is showing and its complete window-relative
bounds fit the preview, rather than treating an overscan node as visible. Final
large captures were inspected with the full object/reason/ID/size readable.
The IME cases explicitly enable the producer's text-input-v3 global, observe
seven-byte uncommitted preedit, cancel it by Tab focus transfer, return focus,
commit Unicode and explicitly Apply. Candidate rectangles are nonempty. All
positive hosts close normally; the provider-disabled negative maps and fails
discovery as intended, which is not normal-close evidence. The driver still
requires every original keyboard command, plus all four new controlled IME
commands where applicable, within the original 50-second limit.

An initial ASan small-dialog run exposed overwritten eager text layouts during
the added internal focus/close passes. Freeing eager layouts before each rebuild
and the repeated-small-dialog regression close that leak. Same-batch Escape also
exposed an invalid second close of an already dismissed scope; dismissal now uses
the SDK's existing omission contract. A deliberately faulty eligibility build
using filtered unknown counts fails the hidden-unknown assertion; the normal
source/library are never replaced by this mutant. Earlier focus-precondition,
disabled-producer, command-count and unsupported screen-coordinate probe failures
are retained. Earlier successful overscan-only checks are superseded by the final
window-relative visibility checks. No deadline, assertion or instrumentation was
relaxed to obtain these results.

Source/revision archives, flags, final binaries/SDK provenance, JUnit/logs, all
native campaigns, full images, negative sources and a SHA-256 manifest are stored
at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-removal-filter-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`d05cdcf3f746858b24a84fa2bbc26106b05f1944e7ed2791bff5175ec1af53f3`.
Twelve compiled consumer/build/runner files match the implementation revision
after CRLF/LF normalization. The compositor binary remains
`631ec1740d9de6daac790d2c29351df7ccb2cabce936ac6ab35ba59b8cb71c75`.
Earlier failed host hashes/logs and source archives are retained; overwritten
earlier host binaries are not claimed to be retained. These standalone examples
are not automatically executed by root SDK CI. The separate [base CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42298896089)
has successful sanitizer and four Windows/macOS jobs, with both Ubuntu jobs
cancelled; it is not a complete seven-job result and the cancellation cause is
not established here.

Aetheri can reuse this composition against the tested 0.1.23 SDK with its existing
owner boundaries while developing independently. Its currently documented 0.1.18
pin is not upgraded here, and actual application adoption is not declared.
Model refresh/reconciliation, filtering complete real
application inventories, retention editing, authenticated plan transport, actual
removal/recovery, installed IMEs, real locales, other platforms and physical/human
acceptance remain open. The Files unpaced sanitizer burst failure is unchanged.
This advances the filter/dialog slice of L04/L05/L09/A04/I01/D07/P02/P04/Q04;
neither those entire requirements nor the 103-ID catalogue are declared complete.

## Standard native text in operation and removal views (2026-11-06)

Implementation source [72e756502ed2e68815ee314fe5ece0ae8881287d](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/commit/72e756502ed2e68815ee314fe5ece0ae8881287d)
builds on `5190a8d2193d65f9113917850232e3cfa94dc402`. Inspection found that
operation metadata and removal-plan/object values were published only as ordinary
semantic values: Linux exposed them through `nexaui-value`, without a standard
Text interface. This was an example-composition gap; the installed SDK already
provides `nx_semantics_add_readonly_text_snapshot_without_caret`.

Both examples now reuse that API. The operation view publishes its nine metadata
rows; small and virtualized removal views publish metadata, six category counts
and current object values. Names remain labels, values retain exact unseparated
identities and complete reasons/sizes, and status nodes keep their existing status
contract. Snapshot identities follow the stable row IDs within each view. No
caret, editable interface, operation permission or writable selection is invented.
View replacement retires the original context and its native text references.
SDK implementation, public API/ABI, version 0.1.23, dependencies, original Aetheri
source/model and consumer pin remain unchanged.

On the isolated Fedora 44/GCC 16.2.1 guest, static Release, shared Release and
static ASan/UBSan Debug each passed the three removal CTests and the original-
catalogue observer regression. This includes the 100,000-object workload and nine
constructor-allocation failure points. Existing 30/15-second CTest deadlines,
leak detection and sanitizer halt-on-error remain unchanged. These are standalone
installed consumers, not newly added root SDK CI tests.

Independent libatspi clients, without NexaUI linkage, read complete standard Text
alongside the existing diagnostic attributes. They check character count, caret
offset -1, no selections, no EditableText interface, rejected caret/selection
changes and unchanged text afterward. The removal client checks all eight small
objects' exact reasons, IDs and logical sizes, global large-plan counts/identities
and the final object reached with native keys. Both clients retain a Text
reference across view replacement and require an error with no old/new text from
that retired reference.

All twelve positive native cases passed on the pinned Aetheri headless compositor:
Release and ASan/UBSan each cover removal native actions, large-list keys,
offscreen unknown-state gating and small-view actions, plus observer actions and
keys. Existing exact request/no-replay, busy-to-unknown, focus, original catalogue
invariants and normal-close checks remain in force. Two AT-SPI-disabled hosts map
but fail their original discovery gate as expected. The two pre-change hosts
instead fail the strengthened clients specifically because standard Text is
missing. These four deliberate negatives do not count as normal-close evidence.

There are 63 complete byte-identical PPM pairs: 51 software captures across the
three profiles and twelve native output captures. For the software comparison,
the exact base view sources were compiled with each candidate's flags and linked
against the same unchanged test objects and SDK; all six baseline executables
passed. Native captures match the pinned preceding removal/observer captures.
Thirteen original consumer source/header files were checked against the exact
base archive. All nineteen compiled consumer/build/runner source files match the
implementation revision after CRLF/LF normalization. An initial comparison used
an older historical observer image and differed; it was replaced by the exact-base
comparison, with the mismatch retained. A long output path also prevented one

Short, isolated evidence paths resolved that harness issue without changing SDK,

## Large declared removal preview (2026-11-06)

On base `4b1e79188f385bc560876a480cfd0d0321898b4c`, the
[removal composition](../../examples/28_removal_review/README.md) now copies up to
100,000 declared objects instead of embedding a 32-object array. This is an
example-only source change: `removal_plan.entries` becomes a borrowed input span;
the installed SDK API/ABI, development version 0.1.23 and Aetheri remain unchanged.
The owner validates every copied entry and both identity namespaces, stores global
disposition counts and preserves caller order within each group. Unknown objects
outside the viewport still block the request. Oversized or invalid input is
rejected in full. The record copy occupies 45,600,000 bytes for the declared
100,000-object Linux/x86-64 corpus; indexes, fonts and total process RSS are separate.

Large views reuse `nx_virtual_list_rebuild`, range/offset queries and measured
height updates. Text layouts are created for visible/overscan rows, with a hard
256-row cache through one public frame call. Intermediate geometry settles before
the call returns; a staged request is committed to its owner only after success.
The six global counts and exact plan/installation/scope identities remain in the
native semantic tree, alongside the current object range. Small views keep their
full tree. The tested workload peaked at 26 semantic nodes, ten row-layout
constructions per call and four settling passes; these are corpus observations,
not universal maxima or adopted latency budgets. Estimated heights remain until
visited; direct offscreen native lookup and filtering are still open.

GCC 16.2.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Debug each passed
all three standalone CTests without skips. The 100,000-object case exercises
Home/End, 20 viewport/font combinations, 64 wheel-driven frames, exact last-object
identity, whole-snapshot duplicates, an offscreen unknown object, and a once-only
request during geometry changes followed by view replacement. Nine injected
owner/view-constructor allocation failures preserve input, operation state and
live-allocation balance. The original 30/15-second limits remain unchanged;
leak detection and sanitizer halt-on-error remain enabled.

An independent libatspi client passed six large native cases on Aetheri's isolated
headless compositor: Release and ASan/UBSan each cover native scrollbar actions,
virtual Shift-Tab/End/Tab plus request/close keys, and a far-end unknown object
that blocks the initial view. It verifies the exact last object, fewer than 300
preview children, retired references and no request replay. Small-view assistive
regressions passed in both profiles as well. All eight positive hosts exited
normally. A provider-disabled large host mapped and failed discovery at the
expected review node. Final native Release/ASan host SHA-256 values are
`19acef79c6e0c0893ad059f2bd4239bdfd4c46fa5c3e2bb42c69f9108b751bee` and
`827c063f475fb0254c3fe5a65c7c82793f6c3fbc57c3c81e7e38a0df294f2bec`;
the independent client is
`f8c3619e09745371aba9f880177077303bf57ae17c5623cf151ee43d462938ab`.
The inspected native End capture hashes to
`0583e71b5fff6a380942cd1b4f95aac092a5e33285b928f4dba32c7c8465d263`.
An earlier large fixture mixed category and reason templates; image review found
this and the final fixture uses matching reasons. Initial data/results are retained.

The first geometry-settling implementation reshaped the same rows repeatedly.
Three fixed alternating before/after process pairs with identical final input
reduced the maximum row-layout work from 34 to 10. Their 64-frame per-process
medians ranged 17.032–17.321 ms before and 10.465–10.497 ms after. These QEMU-guest
frame-construction/validation timings exclude rasterization, presentation and
assistive IPC; they are not SDL comparisons or end-to-end responsiveness evidence.
The cache lasts only through the call. Full before/after RGBA and semantic files
match in 22 pairs, including the actual request-returning frame, two themes,
normal/doubled text, both viewport ends and reopened state. Eleven original small-
view images are also byte-identical. Wrong-object indexing and unknown eligibility
mutants fail their intended large-model assertions; suppressing cached-status
invalidation changes the request-returning pixels and is detected. The initial
restoration guard compared whole `.a` files, whose member timestamps vary here;
the restored object instead matches the separately built/native-tested object
byte-for-byte. Its hash is
`4770bf69aa22b958ea19890bc8ace8f9fb8d113c4a03ff4643b365b7896b8326`.

Source, final binaries/flags/JUnit/native JSON, original and final data/results,
negative variants, paired logs, complete pixel/semantic files, SDK artifacts and
manifest are archived at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-removal-virtual-20261106-v2.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `d9ab0211c58b3f834ba9f58433d63185d34d75fa0f12c8c42b56c58f96c4d498`.
The test clock retains Linux monotonic timing and has a C17 fallback for platforms
without that API; the large workload was rebuilt and passed in all three profiles

Real authenticated inventory/plan transport, retention editing, actual removal,

## Declared removal-plan review composition (2026-11-06)

Based on `4eed8f0373ba33e490cb4307339e1b8fc338feee`, the external installed
C17 [removal review example](../../examples/28_removal_review/README.md) composes
existing text, scrolling, checkbox, button, focus and semantics APIs. A copied
owner-supplied plan distinguishes removal, shared dependency retention, deliberate
retention, independent documents, backup/remote copies and unknown/unmanaged state.
It preserves exact opaque plan/installation/scope/object identities, revision,
reasons and logical sizes; the latter do not promise physically reclaimed space.
All six groups remain explicit even when empty. The first bounded composition
accepts up to 32 declared objects and rejects a larger input without truncation.
Large inventory paging/virtualization remains an implementation/adoption gap.

Incomplete, unknown, invalidated or empty-removal plans cannot be acknowledged or
requested. For an eligible plan the user explicitly reviews its scope; the view
returns one exact request correlation to its retained owner. That is neither
authorization nor an acceptance/completion receipt. A replaced view remains in
the unknown-outcome state and cannot emit the request again. Close closes only
the view. The immutable Aetheri `1fb45c7c21f914a2893457132b609be50ef4280d` ownership
documents require these distinctions, but its source has no full removal-plan
API. This example therefore uses declared fixtures, not an actual removal backend.

GCC 16.2.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Debug each passed
the standalone CTest with an explicit real-font input. Cases cover copied caller
data, complete exact request IDs, invalid UTF-8/unterminated fields, duplicate IDs,
oversized input, stale queued actions, empty/incomplete/unknown plans, 32 objects,
Tab/Enter/Space, vertical and long-name horizontal scrolling, and view replacement
without replay. Two mutations (rearming at view creation and allowing unknown
objects) failed their expected assertions. Dark/light, normal/doubled text and
640×480/420×320 software matrices rendered with semantic and control-bound checks;
representative captures were inspected. Visual identity grouping and checkbox
spacing were corrected after the first image review.

On the isolated Aetheri test compositor from
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90`, six final native cases passed:
Release and ASan/UBSan each exercised independent libatspi actions, virtual
keyboard input verified through native focus/checked state, and an incomplete-plan
Close path. The client verifies all six groups, all eight declared objects, exact
plan/installation/scope values, disabled-action rejection, retired native request
references and no replay after view replacement. Positive hosts exited normally;
leak detection and sanitizer halt-on-error stayed enabled. A separate provider-
disabled host mapped, then failed at native review discovery as expected. The
initial driver invocation selected a Weston-only wrapper and exited 77 before
mapping; it is retained, and the correct generic private-registry runner is used
without weakening that wrapper's guards.

Final native Release/ASan host SHA-256 values are
`5286d40412d0f37da2f02caaa47202388b58ff8c1a901db49b2a5494c4ac0ee7` and
`dd87d76686663ac4b33e8abfee6535bbfb3dd90e441a2836ce5f0d91a1461acc`;
the independent client is
`084807e98a605019924da837d1fe7fa9b1bbd59ea1f42f2115a1b71f070dc135`.
The native 1920×1080 output capture was inspected and hashes to
`c9e3c055bdeb83b22630c34c55fba56b228aa5d236a36c097fbfe265420d0772`.
All 100 production include/src/platform files in the installed SDK's source
snapshot match the base revision byte-for-byte. No SDK implementation, public API,
version, dependency, original Aetheri source or consumer pin changed.

Real authenticated plan transport, larger inventories, retention-choice editing,

Separately, base [4eed8f0 CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42275148202)

## PTY readiness snapshot in the transfer fixture (2026-11-06)

On base `2a21fbd109499813a0102fa03b00487670562cca`, the
[Ubuntu static CI job](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42272333944/job/128079788438)
failed the transfer fixture's assertion that a later queued write must still make
no progress after filling a PTY and observing it as non-writable. The original
fixture passed 20 consecutive local runs. A diagnostic retaining the original
assertion but delaying that later attempt by 5ms reproduced the failure: the
initial SDK readiness flags were zero, a fresh raw poll reported `POLLOUT`, and
the actual write accepted all 26 bytes before any slave reader was started.
The SDK's earlier readiness observation does not promise an unchanged descriptor
state at a later write; see the Linux [poll contract](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/poll.2.html).

The test now limits its first real queue attempt to three bytes, checks either
no progress or a valid partial write including offset, remaining bytes and cleared
prefix, then resumes and verifies the complete byte stream through the actual
PTY. A new `nexaui_wayland_poll_many_transfer_pty_early_writable` variant explicitly
reads and validates filler bytes before that attempt, waits for writable readiness,
and requires progress. Its later stream check accounts for every early-read byte.
Restoring the old no-progress assertion rejects this variant while the SDK archive
remains byte-identical. Both default and early-writable cases are required by the
CI result checker. Existing error injection, bounded queue, delayed reader, socket
backpressure, transfer deadlines and the 15-second CTest limit are retained.

GCC 16.2.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Debug each passed
all five `poll_many` cases on the isolated Aetheri headless test compositor:
the two transfer cases, ordinary group wait, allocator reentry and peer destruction.
There were no skips. Leak detection and sanitizer halt-on-error remained enabled;
the two ASan/UBSan transfer cases additionally passed five repetitions each.
An initial run without a compositor skipped the three native neighbors; those
results are retained separately and are not acceptance. Builds initially reported
the carrier's source timestamp skew; only the extracted private source file times
were normalized for subsequent builds, without changing its clock or source bytes.

No production source, public API, version, dependency or Aetheri file changed.
This closes the demonstrated PTY test assumption, not the separate unpaced Files
queue-exhaustion stress failure or complete terminal/performance adoption.
Source snapshot, candidate patch, original/diagnostic/negative logs, binaries,
build flags, JUnit and SHA-256 manifest are archived at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-pty-fixture-race-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`a953371d2cae847b0413ce137eb093b802e32a506dd9bf2cc7f17b3013a5f7a7`.

## Clipboard fixture producer synchronization (2026-11-06)

Based on `962b3f5ce54a3de36c71083a458828abe85ed6cd`, two retained CI failures
were reproduced without changing the SDK: the stalled self-destroy descriptor
assertion from [060e2fe](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42256940805)
and the Settings stalled-descriptor/read-count assertion from
[eff6efe](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42263483748).
The private producer writes bytes before recording its retained writer FD. A
reader can consume those bytes and return while the producer is preempted before
that atomic store. A test-only 50ms delay at exactly that point made both original
assertions fail in 0.00/0.01 seconds. The original non-delayed baseline passed.
Before and after the diagnostic/candidate test changes, the static SDK archive
remained byte-identical, SHA-256
`504ce46a1f8dfed604e4bd4cb3c538409b2df424af29fae7a12df7bb8060cf9b`.

The self-destroy test now joins its already terminating producer before checking
and closing the retained FD. It explicitly destroys remaining server-side clients
before destroying the display. The first sanitizer run of the delayed case
exposed a 19,192-byte/31-allocation private-server leak when teardown stopped the
loop before it processed client disconnect; that failed output is retained.
Explicit fixture client destruction removed it, with leak checking still enabled.
The SDK window allocator's zero-live check and the original FD-close assertion
are unchanged.

Settings' old twenty 100ms polls could all return immediately with queued events;
iteration count was not elapsed time or producer completion. Its setup now waits
on the actual atomic producer state within the same intended two-second budget,
pumps the selected poll API nonblocking and yields briefly to the producer.
The existing <1.0/<1.5-second call checks, five-second SDK transfer deadline,
model values, exact read counts, ticket/lease assertions and CTest limits remain
unchanged. The fix does not add SDK retries or extend a transfer deadline.

Two permanent delayed-record cases exercise the scheduling edge and are required
by `check_wayland_results.py`. Fedora/GCC 16.2.1/CMake 4.3.0/Wayland 1.26.0/
xkbcommon 1.13.1 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan each passed
33 selected Settings clipboard/async and multi-window lifecycle cases: 33/33,
zero failures and zero skips per final JUnit. The new delayed self-destroy case
also passed five consecutive sanitizer runs. `ASAN_OPTIONS` retained
`detect_leaks=1:halt_on_error=1`; UBSan halted on errors. Release assertions use
the existing explicit `CHECK` macro. No production source, public API, package
version or Aetheri source/pin was changed.

Full candidate source, configurations, compiler/link flags, baseline and delayed
failures, initial sanitizer leak, final logs/JUnit, binary hashes and parsed
summary are archived at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-clipboard-fixture-race-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`f973c90505026bd0672e6315f05dbdca725b6df7ded249caabe209b117f01a27`.
The builder-declared source revision was empty; this identifies the cited base
plus these test changes, not a release attestation.

This advances Q02/P05/I05/W10 evidence reliability for those two race assertions.
Full CI remains a separate gate. In particular,
[962b3f5 CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42267766951) separately
failed the PTY backpressure assertion at
`tests/test_wayland_poll_many_transfer.c:111`; it is not corrected by this change.
The unpaced Files sanitizer stress gate and physical/human acceptance remain
open. The earlier X11 window-creation failure's precise cause also remains
unproven; this producer race must not be used to explain unrelated failures.

## Native original-operation observer (2026-11-06)

On base `eff6efecea02c8a7584a5696116d5be0e94806e0`, the
[external observer](../../examples/27_aetheri_operation/README.md) adds a public
Wayland/software host and an independent C17 libatspi client. The host waits on
native/accessibility work and a borrowed test-control pipe using
`nx_window_poll_with_fd`; it creates no activity animation timer. A fixture
command transitions the same original operation to uncertain, detaches the old
semantics and recreates only the view. Catalogue, request, manifest and fonts
stay with the original test owner. No SDK core or Aetheri source changed.

The catalogue/core libraries remain Aetheri
`1fb45c7c21f914a2893457132b609be50ef4280d`. The isolated headless compositor is
the unchanged test binary from Aetheri
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90`, SHA-256

software SDK builds were installed into separate test prefixes. The native
consumer uses installed public NexaUI headers; the separate libatspi client uses
no NexaUI/Aetheri headers or libraries. Its C17 compile used
`-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror` and the existing private AT-SPI development
prefix. No system packages, live session, SDK pin or authority were changed.

Space/Tab/Enter input, each in Release and ASan/UBSan. The independent client
queries complete operation-ID and component values, status role, busy/read-only
states, absent numeric value/action, cleared busy after view replacement,

host checks one acceptance, one operation and unchanged catalogue revision
during view-only work. The Python driver independently reads the final original

compositor exit zero. This is controlled metadata uncertainty, not a real failed

its old build tree also failed and is retained. Neither fixture issue implied an

## Original catalogue operation observer (2026-11-06)

Based on NexaUI `762ae4c1602948579fd3c945aa7460587b125b80`, the standalone
[C17 observer](../../examples/27_aetheri_operation/README.md) reuses Aetheri
`1fb45c7c21f914a2893457132b609be50ef4280d`'s unchanged catalogue/core libraries,
schema generator, frozen manifest vector and original intent encoder. The view
copies its exact request binding and calls only `ae_catalogue_fixture_get` on
explicit refresh. It presents operation/install/scope/generation/storage/artifact
identities, component and observed revision, with complete semantic hex values
and wrapping visual groups. An unqueried view, failed query, busy observation and
uncertain outcome remain distinct. Historical activation evidence and current
installation state are separate. The busy band has fixed phase and no timer.

The test owner creates a private catalogue and performs original metadata
acceptance/transitions. No payload is installed, published or activated. Tests
cover a missing operation, active metadata, staging-ready without installation
completion, an injected read failure after an
active observation, uncertainty across view replacement, caller draft mutation,
another unaccepted operation and keyboard Refresh/Tab/Close/scroll-End. They
assert one owner acceptance, one operation and unchanged catalogue revision
through view-only work. Isolated mutants that accept on read or retain a fresh
busy presentation after query failure each fail their intended assertion.

On Fedora/GCC 16.2.1/CMake 4.3.0, the existing installed Wayland-only software
0.1.23 static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan SDK profiles each
built and ran the external consumer. Aetheri's external dependencies were
OpenSSL 3.5.9 and SQLite 3.51.2; neither becomes a new NexaUI core dependency.
`ldd` resolved the shared core from the selected 0.1.23 prefix. The profiles
produced matching software frames at 640×480 and 420×320 with dark/light themes
and doubled theme font size. Inspected PNGs are under
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexa-observer-release-layout-run/`. The narrow large-text viewport
is small and scrollable; final density/minimum-size policy remains open. An
initial compiler rejection of an imprecisely bounded formatting call is retained;
the final code formats the fixed-size fields with explicit bounds and passes
`-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror`.

`899f7cf8bffefe93c6d085f03dffb3603565bda8107a21fe18c36eca16fce3d9`.

`D:/NexaUI/out/aetheri-1fb45c7-operation-observer.tar`, SHA-256

This advances D07/D11/L11/P07/B07 as an original-model presentation experiment,
not production installation/recovery acceptance. Native window/input and composed
AT-SPI, publication/activation-specific UI branches, real asynchronous services, trusted routing, durable recovery, locales,
other platforms and human/physical acceptance remain open. The activity control's
separate native checks do not prove the new composition. Aetheri may reuse this
view pattern within its authorized owner; an ordinary UI app must not open an
internal authority catalogue just to render it. No live Aetheri source, SDK pin,
user catalogue or ongoing work was modified.

Separately, [060e2fe CI](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42256940805)
passed six desktop jobs but its sanitizer reverse-driver pass failed an existing
`test_wayland_multi_failure.c:70` stalled-descriptor assertion. The subsequent

sanitizers and five desktop jobs; Ubuntu static was cancelled during dependency
installation before configure/build. These are not complete seven-job acceptance
results, and neither proves resolution of the intermittent descriptor failure.
The separate unpaced Files sanitizer stress gate also remains open.

## Private X11 server lifetime during accessibility tests (2026-11-06)

The Ubuntu static job in [run 42256285606](https://github.com/Lulus792/NexaUI/actions/runs/42256285606)
on `1687ea3a4ebe7b7a45a77591223e6d508e040fe2` failed
`nexaui_atspi_multi_session_discovery_private`: the host could not create a new
native window after a callback destroyed the previous one. Its other 242 tests
passed. The original Linux artifact is retained at
`D:/NexaUI/out/activity-1687-ci-linux-static.zip`, SHA-256
`20b62062f746d03def52034f3a67491eca176ce5bb46f5aff192b083decc6a9c`.

On Alpine-Dev, the unchanged test from current source
`060e2fe394394e984fd3c495647bae8010337eb4` passed five consecutive runs.
Thus the original window-creation failure has not been reproduced locally and
its precise cause remains unproven. The subsequent Ubuntu static CI job on
060e2fe also passed. Neither result erases the earlier failure.

A separate Xlib probe showed that the private Xvfb fixture resets its root
property state whenever its last client closes. This was unwanted during tests
that deliberately destroy their sole window and immediately create another.
The fixture now requests `-noreset`; it still owns and terminates that private
server. The [new native guard](../../tests/xvfb_lifetime_probe.c) makes 64
independent connections and verifies the same root property survives between
client lifetimes. The old runner fails this guard at cycle 1; the candidate
passes. No SDK retry, application assertion or existing timeout was changed.
This demonstrates correction of the fixture reset, not proof that it caused the
intermittent CI window-creation error.

On the Debug static X11 build, the original multiselection test passed five runs
with the candidate fixture. The ordinary native AT-SPI, session discovery,
disabled bridge and missing-runtime-ABI cases passed 4/4. An initial neighboring
run lacked the unbuilt fake D-Bus ABI library and failed that case; building the
existing fixture dependency and rerunning the same four tests passed. The
original failed output is retained. A preliminary 500-cycle exploratory probe
exceeded the fixture's existing 60-second limit and was not used as a pass;
the final guard has the bounded 64-cycle contract above.

Source changes from 060e2fe, CMake cache, baseline/candidate/negative logs and
the original CI ZIP are archived at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-xvfb-lifetime-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`89316a5b3c2047caf41f2d0630c1a5ba70bc12220155c63c1b6be13cb5982fed`.
This is Q02/Q03/P05 test-infrastructure evidence; no new UI capability,
production X11 change, Aetheri change or Files stress fix is claimed.

## Unquantified operation activity (2026-11-06)

Based on NexaUI `7998ca5d95ece2b7440020434f025f5aa7291a49`, this 0.1.23
development candidate adds `nx_activity` without changing `nx_progress` or an
existing descriptor. Aetheri's management operation response can supply a
state/phase/outcome without a completion fraction; the new control exposes a
read-only busy status, copied name/description and no numeric value or actions.
Its `[0,1]` phase positions a visual band only. Scheduling and operation ownership
remain with the caller; constant phase supports reduced motion. An unknown
result is a separate observed status, not an inferred busy or completed state.
The [external C17 example](../../examples/26_operation_status/README.md) renders
measured progress, unquantified activity and an unknown result without a display,
GPU, network, timer or operation backend.

On the Fedora carrier (GCC 16.2.1, CMake 4.3.0, Wayland 1.26.0, xkbcommon 1.13.1),
static Release, shared Release and static ASan/UBSan Wayland-only software
profiles with AT-SPI disabled each passed `nexaui_cloud_components`. Checks cover
invalid phases, busy/no-value semantics, band geometry, rasterization and
allocation rollback with a successful retry in the same context. Each installed
package built and ran the external C17 example with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror`.
CMake rejected the older 0.1.22 package. `ldd` confirmed the shared consumer used
the new installation. All three 480×180 PPMs had SHA-256
`6f8c7fd2a41f8282f3c46f12c7be487e10a42fd48ab200950f2490bc06093949`;
the inspected conversion is `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-activity-20261106.png`.

Separate AT-SPI-enabled static Release and ASan/UBSan builds ran the existing
Linux accessibility host/client suite on Aetheri's isolated test compositor from
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90`. The extended independent libatspi
client checked the activity role, busy/read-only states, description, absent
value/action interfaces and absent `nexaui-value` attribute. A host command then
replaced that same status identity with an unknown result; the client observed
its new description/name and cleared busy state. Both complete suites passed and
closed cleanly. An isolated host mutant omitting busy failed the intended state
assertion; its forced negative teardown is not a clean-close result. This tests
native queries and a controlled transition, not human screenreader announcements
or real service reconciliation. The first manual mutant compile omitted the
existing test-hook definition and failed; adding that fixture definition allowed
the intended negative runtime check, without changing SDK code.

Native Release and sanitizer host hashes were respectively
`d62258f7fe478fdac3c355fb898337419e2c16d7cffe27c65fc913a9b3426892` and
`dc86904b3683e11f493cd910cca15d6f0a4aac94aa213a3fb2c4c60ee6024b8c`.
Source, CMake caches, test/example logs, native results, mutant and image outputs
are archived at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-activity-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`2255947028ede155469bbf5e67af3fe4c772e6c2d0de8a5a7966965161059e18`.
Builder-declared source revision was empty; the archive identifies the candidate
from the cited base plus these API/test changes rather than claiming a release
attestation. The font is the carrier's existing Liberation Sans Regular.

This advances D07/D11/L11/A02 presentation seams. It does not close P01/P07,
real installer/device/software commands, exact-target review, durable reconnect,
trusted authorization, production accessibility, other platforms, localization,
physical input/scaling or human design/assistive acceptance. The separate Files
unpaced sanitizer stress failure remains open. Aetheri may adopt the new control
independently at 0.1.23; no Aetheri source, SDK pin or live session was changed.
No 1.0.0 release or tag is implied.

On source `1687ea3a4ebe7b7a45a77591223e6d508e040fe2`, a test-only follow-up
advances visual phase while keeping the semantic status unchanged. The independent
client receives no object events for that identity, then receives exactly one
busy-clear event when the host replaces it with the unknown result. Both complete
native Release and ASan/UBSan suites passed. A separate host mutant that changed
the description on a phase update failed the zero-event assertion. This covers
native event traffic for the fixture, not human announcement behavior or measured
idle/animation performance. SDK implementation and package remain unchanged.
Sources, native logs/results, binary hashes and mutant are archived at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-activity-events-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`ec9ae40220d2983a4b8a913d84a8724d4937839a399bf1428522541bb11d30f0`.

example changes the filter's accessible description when a whole query is
rejected and restores it after Clear. If validation changes during a frame,
the host rebuilds once before publishing so that the description and current
admission state are from the same update. This uses the existing styled-field
and semantic event APIs; it does not add a second model or change Aetheri.

`accessible-description` property-change events for the same filter object.

IME focus-cancel case and the unpaced Release Files regression. A separate
mutant suppressing only description events failed the event assertion. These
checks cover English example strings and a private registry; locale switching,
production accessibility services, Windows/macOS and human review remain open.
The separate unpaced sanitizer stress gate also remains open.

## Files model/view on the Aetheri compositor and burst-input finding (2026-11-06)

The external Files experiment, based on NexaUI
`6d8df09131a5728b165036147cf5b4398de6b893`, was built against the installed
static Wayland-only SDK from `861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`
and the unchanged Files model and test compositor from Aetheri
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90`. On a private 20-file directory,
the Release run rendered a frame, used native keyboard navigation and refresh,
filtered to `item01`, rejected an overlong query without adopting its rejected
suffix, retained the last accepted model query, and closed with all file names
and contents unchanged. The compositor and consumer exited 0.

The same unpaced 256-key burst is **not closed under sanitizers**: several
GCC 16.2.1 ASan/UBSan runs lost the client connection and exited 1 before the
full draft was processed. A diagnostic run localized the observed failure to
software rendering returning `NX_ERROR_PLATFORM` with last `errno=EPIPE`.
The later isolated investigation below identified server-side queue exhaustion
in one failing run. Another instrumented run
with 2 ms between injected keys passed, as did an unpaced Release run. The
default driver remains unpaced; the paced case is a separate comparison, not
a replacement for the failing stress case. An intermediate test incorrectly
expected the original six-byte filter rather than the most recently accepted
prefix and timed out; that harness failure is also preserved.

The final Release consumer SHA-256 was
`5d0ff070eac5d0c48d72f776ecc3fbd9fc5b904756e5bf586b5a36614d4b3c9d`,
and the final instrumented consumer was
`9fa46ed33615c8f2c292b6eac785b855216d0e3b7143827ae3527c14c446f14d`.
Logs, captures, diagnostic attempts, source and build caches are archived at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-files-native-aetheri-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`cc9410841050475e650cd820109512d5bc60c4833544511ba313bd472ab4c952`.
This is an example using real model code and a headless test compositor, not
the production Files application. Resolution of the burst failure, native
assistive acceptance, physical input and actual Aetheri adoption remain open.

### Burst queue investigation

On the same Aetheri test compositor and input driver, a separately rebuilt
diagnostic SDK and Files consumer logged `wl_display_flush` failing with
`EPIPE` while `wl_display_get_error` still returned zero. A preload diagnostic
on the compositor exposed its normally suppressed libwayland 1.26.0 log:
`Data too big for buffer (4080 + 24 > 4096)` followed by `error in client
communication`. The compositor had repeatedly received `EAGAIN` when sending
events; its client was destroyed before the Files process observed `EPIPE`.
This establishes exhaustion of the server's 4096-byte connection buffer in
that run, rather than establishing a general cause for every earlier failure.

For comparison only, another preload diagnostic set the test compositor's
default per-client buffer to 65536 bytes before client creation. With the
same unpaced driver and diagnostic consumer, the entire overlong draft was
processed and both processes exited 0. An explicit 4096-byte comparison also
passed once, so the outcome is timing dependent. Neither comparison changes the
normal test, proves production behavior, or closes the sanitizer stress gate;
larger buffers and a slower input stream are not substituted for a fix. The
diagnostic consumer SHA-256 was
`25ec16dffa001089f172da4f9e317302d38d1d158777d3c98c01d2c027a98110`.
The diagnostic sources, result files and logs are preserved in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-files-burst-investigation-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`f5a1cd1cb111ac08811e70d57a85bd3f7812e925f5feba6f352afd5a0c0044db`.
The next step is to investigate event handling and rendering responsiveness
without altering Aetheri's live compositor or weakening the unpaced test.

An alternating ten-run diagnostic matrix narrowed the delay to the software
presentation path. With the same unpaced driver, Aetheri test compositor and
ASan/UBSan diagnostic SDK, all five runs of the ordinary Files consumer failed
with `EPIPE` at render. A deliberately nonfunctional mutant that presented its
first frame but skipped every later `nx_software_view_render` call passed all
five runs, including the whole-draft and unchanged-file checks. This removes
buffer creation, rasterization and submission together, so it does not isolate
one of those operations and is not a candidate fix or evidence of visible
updates. No preload hook or enlarged buffer was used in this matrix. The
ordinary and mutant executable hashes were respectively
`25ec16dffa001089f172da4f9e317302d38d1d158777d3c98c01d2c027a98110` and
`4015d2416d8cc9447c0549eb75c8ff3d5bf2ebc33a9fdbee69639e37dffed651`.
The scripts, mutated source, build cache and all ten logs/results are archived
at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-files-raster-matrix-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`791d94dc5fbd38df211f3a7a60762251a6edc937bdd03fd7f48d0eea0ec47fa6`.
An actual solution must keep visible updates, input ordering, focus/IME and
bounded resource lifetimes while retaining the original stress case.

### Software stroke raster cost

On base `b00fa4451fc4956a4e8f0d0150a1b56844bbf247`, the candidate changes the
scalar renderer and parity test. A separately instrumented SDK derived from
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`, built with GCC 16.2.1 ASan/UBSan,
attributed the largest measured command cost in a paced Files run to rectangle
strokes. The scalar renderer
now skips the interior pixels of a stroke instead of testing them one by one;
the same clipped outer pixels still use the original blending operation. A
reference-renderer comparison includes 240 additional combinations of
fractional, clipped, extremely thin, thick and translucent strokes. The
headless parity and renderer backend tests both passed under ASan/UBSan and in
a separate Release build. Release output is saved at
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-stroke-release.log`.

Two consecutive diagnostic runs, comparing that instrumented `861ac66` SDK
before and after substituting the candidate's `headless.c`, with a 2 ms key
delay measured median
software-raster times of 5.678 ms (53 frames, unmodified renderer) and
2.077 ms (66 frames, optimized renderer). They used the same virtual Files
input, framebuffer and carrier, but naturally produced different frame counts;
these are scoped diagnostic measurements, not a general performance guarantee.
Both paced runs passed. The optimized consumer still failed all five separate
unpaced ASan/UBSan runs with `EPIPE` during presentation: the queue-exhaustion
stress gate is therefore **not fixed**. The first captured frame in that run
had the same SHA-256 as the baseline capture. The headless carrier reported
clock skew between archived source timestamps and its clock during builds;
these were fresh build directories, and the warning is preserved in the logs.
The candidate source, configuration, failures, comparisons and test output are
archived at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-stroke-raster-20261106-complete.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `88d0b1914ebf77ba0072f3daa45aad4d2393d850496acdf1be6798de826d563e`.

## External clipboard peers and interrupted read on Aetheri (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `8ed604eb222a893be8052929eebdc97d736ce67e`, the isolated
Aetheri `d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90` test compositor and
separate NexaUI test processes exchanged Unicode in both directions with the
unrelated `wl-clipboard` 2.2.1 tools: `wl-paste` read the exact NexaUI-owned
selection and NexaUI read a distinct exact value owned by `wl-copy`. A new
read was rejected after the foreign owner was terminated. The Alpine tool and
its musl runtime were copied into a private VM directory; no system package or
live Aetheri worktree was changed. Tool SHA-256 values: `wl-paste`
`7221ecacd258bf00989f30bff9ea02bc3cac17a588150f4bf81a8b0253ef9356`,
`wl-copy` `75f5428d5a36c0c9d06611d4020f580a4622400e2f64579ecb45644dc888a921`.

In a separate case, the test stopped its NexaUI source process after it owned
the selection, observed the recipient's pending read and the compositor's
`wl_data_source.send`, then killed only that stopped source. The recipient's
read returned `NX_OK` with zero bytes before a later new read was rejected.
That EOF is indistinguishable from a legitimate empty value; a partial transfer
could similarly look like a valid prefix. The public clipboard documentation
now states this limitation explicitly. This result is not evidence that the
original value was delivered or that every owner-loss ordering is detectable.

Both directions and the interrupted-read observation passed GCC 16.2.1 Release
and ASan/UBSan using the installed static Wayland-only SDK based on
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`. The killed source cannot report
leaks at exit; the surviving instrumented receiver and normal foreign-peer
runs exited without sanitizer reports. Instrumented peer SHA-256:
`4f138eba1cafaa50e155b173f67a6988b32f55a7333ea067e34add3fe4af2865`;
release peer SHA-256:
`cdb13ffd19ddf4112540f08193c9fe81570b734d20a58ab660659b5eb92f898f`.
The first interrupted attempt timed out because its log matcher expected `@`
instead of the server's `#`; that failed attempt is retained. The RPM download
attempt did not complete and was stopped; the existing Alpine tool archive was
used instead. Logs, source and build caches are in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-foreign-clipboard-inflight-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`7fd4dcb382e785adf44ded5d3eb9c48942fbc96b560b2a1f490db118acd2759b`.
Production Aetheri application integration, other clipboard managers, physical
input and human accessibility remain open.

## Clipboard across independent NexaUI processes on Aetheri (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `514c1c485ca132463bf3bd225054f35f12433e54`, two separate
instances of a small public C17 consumer ran against the isolated Aetheri

acquired a native input serial and set a fixed Unicode selection; after focus
changed, the other process read the exact bytes asynchronously. Once the

rejected. Both peers and the compositor exited 0. The two processes run the
same NexaUI-based test consumer; this does not establish interoperability with
an unrelated application's clipboard implementation.

`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`. Instrumented peer SHA-256:

release peer SHA-256:
`dded0ad46f2d2dc67567395888a21efb8bb67bc257e9d7dc304dc6132fc2890d`.

## Clipboard between two NexaUI windows on Aetheri (2026-11-06)

On consumer base `0fba4aa397fb9f6429e701972c9a2b4dc436a33b`, the external
two-window C17 test uses public clipboard APIs on the isolated Aetheri
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90` test compositor. With focus and
a real input serial on A, it sets a fixed Unicode sample; after focus moves to
B, B starts and takes an asynchronous read while `nx_window_poll_many` also
services A's source connection. The bytes match exactly. Both orders of closing
a window passed. When source A closed, B regained focus and an attempted new
read returned `NX_ERROR_STATE`, rather than a stale offer. These are two
separate Wayland connections in one test process, not independent applications.

Both close orders passed GCC 16.2.1 Release and ASan/UBSan with leak detection
in the consumer and its installed static Wayland-only 0.1.21 SDK based on
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`. The instrumented consumer
SHA-256 was `282d6364b280d847dd491c74eaaf4245becf56ef823ce62845c69afa05c4f0bf`;
the release consumer was
`c47cc3c252b3ced929ddec6c386b653c424bec065a8695b6fc60fd63aeda85a1`.
The two existing private Weston two-window tests also passed. Results, logs,
sources and build caches are in `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-two-clipboard-20261106.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `22cca44efcae77e331da0681ed6642ba4cae53e6d67cb0950fa33410e6191a00`;

An in-flight read during owner loss, external peers, production applications,

## Two NexaUI windows on the Aetheri test compositor (2026-11-06)

The unchanged public C17 two-window consumer from NexaUI commit
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6` was linked separately against
that commit's installed static Wayland-only 0.1.21 SDK and run on the isolated
Aetheri `d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90` test compositor. Its
two independent Wayland windows use `nx_window_poll_many` with an application
control descriptor. Virtual pointer input focuses first A and then B; native
keyboard events deliver `a` and `b` to the respective contexts. In separate
runs, the focused A or B is closed by the application. The remaining window
submits another software frame, regains focus and receives `c`; the driver
rejects duplicate or misrouted text. Both processes exit 0.

Both close orders passed GCC 16.2.1 Release and ASan/UBSan with leak detection
in the consumer and static SDK. The instrumented consumer SHA-256 was
`76cf17116f349338e06aacfe1769317c8dede07d21d47e7b7232b851a8bcdb30`;
the release consumer was
`c071bdc101b489fcca4af25a09414b39a56ff58b10da71c029feaf7c898c33a8`.

`075e64ccee41f4abbda1b890c2c10cb9ba301e22737a9d0a4c6d5474130a34b0`.
Frame submission is not proof of presentation. This controlled fixture uses
three virtual 1x outputs and app-owned close; compositor-requested close,
physical displays, human interaction and actual Aetheri app adoption remain
open. No Aetheri worktree was modified.

## Settings Apply worker during compositor loss (2026-11-06)

On example base `c4027c2b34973f99435951d8958d7fd967815c82`, a test-only
inherited pipe holds the Settings example's Apply worker after one keyboard
submission. The isolated Aetheri d56d695 compositor is killed while that
worker is blocked. The driver observes Settings enter cleanup and wait for the
worker, then releases it. Settings joins the worker, destroys its local view
and window, and exits 1. The trace contains one worker start and no published
successful Apply state. This is an example worker and an induced sequence, not
proof of cancellation or durable backend reconciliation after a restart. The
example's worker join is not a general timeout for an unresponsive service.

The case passed with GCC 16.2.1 Release and with ASan/UBSan plus leak detection
on the application and its static Wayland-only 0.1.21 SDK based on
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6`. The normal keyboard workflow
also passed with the same instrumented binary and exited 0. The instrumented
application SHA-256 was
`71a9e2e246e7b134930926965a3b54e4ce2f42eca08b1da5324b1f7a12753374`.
Results, logs, captures, source files and build caches are in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-settings-inflight-loss-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`87756352f0c27a3bff3afac637d8c5b0b21550ccf86d567ae51ae56e90d9e9c5`.
Actual services, other event ordering, Vulkan and accessibility remain open.

## Ordinary Settings after compositor loss (2026-11-06)

An external software Settings example linked to an installed Wayland-only
NexaUI SDK built from `861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6` ran on the
isolated Aetheri `d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90` test compositor.
After a mapped, captured frame and keyboard-ready marker, the driver killed
only that compositor. Settings returned failure (exit 1), reported renderer
retirement incomplete because its connection had failed, and reached a
test-only marker after destroying its scene, window, worker and font. This is
local cleanup, not an acknowledged compositor-side retirement.

The case passed once with a release SDK and again with GCC 16.2.1 ASan/UBSan
instrumenting both Settings and its static SDK, with leak detection enabled
and no sanitizer report. A normal independent run of that same instrumented
Settings binary exercised the keyboard workflow and both processes exited 0.

`9483f2e95a30e05a7231cf32680469c52b9dba55ad1997b7e9b9ddcba9299e10`
and its SDK archive was
`77bc3b03408b6899c2dda0431699f66262a2a5e1668caec101474682b74d6657`.

`006473a8ef9e8af98d17bb8ddf63f93412d83fab35fb33eb5a7bc50c55ce87f9`.
