## Fullscreen over the Aetheri test dock (2026-11-06)

An installed Wayland-only static NexaUI 0.1.21 SDK built from exact commit
`861ac665eeb4662b795629bc502e86fb36a5c9e6` ran an external C17 consumer
on the isolated Aetheri test compositor and private test dock. Both compositor
and test dock were built from the Aetheri snapshot
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90`; the consumer uses public APIs.
On the 1920x1080 virtual output the test observed normal 1840x916, maximized
1920x996, restored 1840x916, fullscreen 1920x1080 and restored 1840x916. It
checks the native fullscreen state and its subsequent exit, captures output
pixels over the dock during fullscreen and verifies the dock reappears after
restore. The consumer, private dock and compositor all exited 0. The original
non-fullscreen dock test also passed. This used GCC 16.2.1, wlroots 0.20.2,
Wayland 1.26.0 and software/pixman rendering, with X11, Vulkan and AT-SPI
disabled in the SDK.

The older Aetheri snapshot `d621c9bba1df8ffd1083999300bcff742022b9dc`
reported fullscreen and the same frame extent, but its dock remained visible
at the sampled bottom pixel; the strict test failed. This is a compositor
version-dependent integration result, not a claim that the SDK controls shell
stacking. Two later attempts on the newer snapshot failed before the fullscreen
check: one compositor reported capture failure and another consumer exited on
SIGBUS while `/tmp` had 800 MiB free. After moving previously captured test
images from tmpfs to the VM disk, the final run passed. Resource pressure is
plausible but the precise cause of those failures was not established.

The tested SDK archive SHA-256 was
`87a8db871377d3423c037523568544593f2ff8f8b011467b6bab95e7d0c6f91c`,
the consumer executable was
`a8e68bd4d0f8c656886998a46cb83f5bdfd7a4366fa6e81a0559e8772f9de260`,
and the final driver source was
`083c37abd9c7edbbd82cce69ad567c15c8ee992ead530368956ae0eac885b5ec`.
Logs, output captures, failed attempts and configuration caches are archived
at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-aetheri-fullscreen-final-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`d8eff76ff18b4baac61060ef5ab682409decea38d3250ea700b5495113c78a27`.
The test dock is a private solid-color fixture, not the production shell.
Physical displays, human acceptance, Windows/macOS native fullscreen and the
live Aetheri worktree remain outside this result.

## Explicit fullscreen request (2026-11-06)

The 0.1.21 candidate on base `3aead3ab3d931af136284d075a515ce82201e4e7`
adds `nx_window_fullscreen` and `NX_WINDOW_CAP_FULLSCREEN`. A successful
request does not assert an observed transition. Wayland requests the
compositor's chosen output and X11 requires the advertised EWMH fullscreen
capability. Windows and macOS return `NX_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED` for a live window;
their fullscreen request implementation and native tests remain open. The
existing restore operation also leaves fullscreen. A restore sent before a
Wayland fullscreen configure sends an explicit fullscreen cancellation.

Alpine/WSL GCC 15.2 static dual-backend tests passed 4/4: private Wayland and
X11 protocol peers plus native Weston and Openbox management tests. They cover
request versus observed state, rejected Wayland request, fullscreen and
restore geometry, and Wayland restore before the first confirmation. A GCC
15.2 Wayland-only shared Release build passed its 2/2 management tests; an
external installed C17 consumer linked and ran with the new symbol. Clang
22.1.3 ASan/UBSan with leak detection passed all 4 dual-backend management
tests. The shared SDK SHA-256 was
`575f10c4ee2333a360ae6e3525efcb124cdb4c3616e96706fb6bc2229a024e3a`;
the sanitized Wayland wire executable was
`52ed995965dcf50819719cf78937d5e3cf9af6c8b87682e2332d3e1d06f5aa4d`.
CTest logs and build caches are archived in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-fullscreen-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`d0a4f5330498617dfa5cccc634e196273c750b0aa4906e7a2808643d446e0708`.
The installed consumer result was separately run with exit 0. Tests use virtual
desktops; actual Aetheri compositor,
physical displays and Windows/macOS builds and human acceptance remain open.
No 1.0.0 release is implied.

## Owner-channel failure in the running desktop fixture (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `fedecf21ce1fce006e8086e07fd876e20f273ab9`, the private
desktop test host rejects malformed or uncorrelated version-3 owner replies
before changing any of its three view models. A failed nonblocking launch or
close send is treated as uncertain: it is not retried and no successful result
is invented. Owner-channel failure reaches local teardown with exit 1, without
claiming acknowledged desktop retirement. Startup and all other failure timing
windows are not covered by this result.

On an isolated Aetheri virtual compositor, uncorrelated, oversized and
ancillary-data replies and owner disconnection all reached the expected
failure teardown. The GCC ASan/UBSan-instrumented host used leak detection;
its shared SDK was not instrumented. An independent AT-SPI client held a
search accessible until owner disconnection and observed it become defunct.
The latter run used a modified 2x compositor fixture. A socketpair regression
also checks divergent view correlation, uncertain sends and normal replies;
the original Aetheri owner, Settings and Terminal passed a separate normal
launch/focus/close run. Before the fix the negative fixture failed to observe
the cleanup marker. The mistaken scale configuration in the evidence archive
timed out and is not counted as a passing test.

The instrumented host SHA-256 is
`a0eb30ab18145cb74f9df4b94135f3a83cd563c2af88599718bfe6c0f2ab4fa9`;
the normal owner run's host SHA-256 is
`2835d5dc0e0aa42d2bf06a75ca16e4cc980be56d6cf50df5726726265ef2e8ea`.
Logs and results are in `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-owner-channel-loss-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `67110e4895caf42377955896b906634768394f973b43def60068cbf800807f7d`.
This is test-host evidence, not production supervisor recovery, physical
hardware or human accessibility acceptance. Aetheri's live worktree was not
changed.

## Unexpected compositor loss in the running desktop fixture (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `ec43b1c29a61393c0da44209fcea8959ff65734b`, the private
desktop test host treats failure of its running Wayland dispatch, flush or
roundtrip as unexpected peer loss. It returns failure after retiring local
views, presenters, pending callbacks and accessibility hosts; it does not
claim an acknowledged desktop retirement. The normal explicit retirement path
remains successful. This covers the event-loop paths exercised here, not every
startup, protocol-error or callback failure path.

With a focused search on an isolated Aetheri test compositor, the driver killed
only that compositor process. Before the change the host exited with an
assertion and did not reach its cleanup marker. Afterward the host reported
the loss and completed local teardown with exit 1. A second test used the
modified 2x compositor fixture, a private AT-SPI registry and an independent
libatspi client that held a search accessible: after peer loss and host
teardown, the old accessible returned an error. The GCC ASan/UBSan-instrumented
host exited 1 with leak detection enabled and no sanitizer report; the SDK
shared library itself was the previously built noninstrumented artifact.
The normal 1x test with independent window and launch peers passed with all
three processes exiting 0. Alpine/WSL focus, IME-wire and launcher regressions
and a separate Clang ASan/UBSan multi-view AT-SPI regression passed.

The sanitized host SHA-256 was
`5942b925a40a42b0a3750db09f776d3d3ac981fb968cae66b0a469a368ed420f`;
the release host was
`bc2d018ba4338f0891ccc891437eed2a9020ceda691f68074c49b371f174888b`.
Logs, the pre-fix failure, an initial attempt where SIGTERM caused a normal
compositor exit, and both successful loss cases are in

position. The client set, read and cleared its text. While the search remained
open, the fixture switched 2x → 1x → 2x; the client independently observed
`(16,16,2528,44)` at 1x and `(16,16,1248,44)` again at 2x, and repeated the
text operation at each scale. The later compact dock transition and logical
pointer-hit checks also passed. A mutant expecting a 2496-pixel semantic width
at 2x failed the independent geometry assertion (exit 1).

The final desktop binary SHA-256 was
`5fedafb1091eae2b7f9dd5ce2a435bae77fb68636d9942626e1079ce37b59839`, the
independent client was
`1816e4fbca6e941e9d24b3130066e8298dba58f13098d669cf15be0f3094a56d`, and the

## Dynamic output scale and configure ordering (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `20456229184ded9ad4d015dd47862a16614567bd`, a controlled
test changed the second virtual output from 2x to 1x and back while its dock
was live. The first run exposed a real ordering issue: a configure for the new
logical width arrived before the corresponding output scale, and the host
submitted a 5120×168 buffer for a 2560×84 logical dock. The host now coalesces
configure and scale changes behind an asynchronous Wayland sync and redraws
afterward. A queued refresh is destroyed when its output is retired.

Deferring the frame also exposed a focus race in the first owner regression:
keyboard enter arrived before the expanded field was built and a first key was
lost. The host now builds and focuses the field at that edge even while the
presentation is deferred. A callback regression checks the first key, and a
controlled Wayland wire test retires an output with its refresh callback pending.

The final Fedora run checked 2x → 1x → 2x backing dimensions, rejected the
previous mixed scale/geometry combinations, captured expected dock pixels after
both transitions, and exercised a 2x logical pointer hit. A separate original
owner run passed Settings, Terminal and native Compose focus/reuse/shutdown on
the unchanged 1x compositor. Static/shared GCC and Clang ASan/UBSan focus,
IME-wire and launcher regressions passed on Alpine/WSL; independent AT-SPI
passed with the sanitizer build. The final test desktop SHA-256 was
`df94c7eed16fa5853a8f15dd170764b0c75dbd9fbfa5b17abcfaa262ff9fb710` and
the modified test compositor was
`01cf280d25f3832b835fd26a3d6d2711ee1a8bc2faad14c811d2f05df1250982`.

The 2x compositor was an isolated Aetheri source fixture with logical layout
and an extra test-only scale command; it is not the unchanged Aetheri
compositor. The initial wrong-buffer run, intermediate focus failure, two
capture assertions affected by the hovered button, final pass, modified fixture
sources and logs are preserved in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-output-dynamic-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`497970cf66958cb88ed07beb119937ba20403ab636584a556099609972724c30`.
Native fractional scale, physical hotplug, the real Aetheri 2x compositor and
broader reconfiguration orderings remain open.

## Integer output scale in the private desktop host (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `25db24712ab1466f2a7fc0f8275063f3eb8ed2a2`, the private

to the output matched by name. It marks only the corresponding view dirty,

dimensions and matching scale to the existing shared-memory presenter. Layout,
input and accessibility geometry remain logical. The host accepts integer
scales 1–8 and rejects frames exceeding the presenter's 128 MiB buffer limit;
this is a bounded test host, not fractional-scale support.

On Fedora, an isolated compositor fixture based on Aetheri source
`d56d69523c33a48648af343e7d36526f84518c90` advertised a 2x second virtual

output contained the expected dock pixels, and a pointer click in logical
coordinates produced the second dock's Search intent. The other two virtual
outputs remained 1x. Two fixture runs passed; the final run also checked that
pointer hit. This altered compositor is a test fixture, not an Aetheri fix or
evidence that its unchanged 2x compositor works.

The unchanged compositor's 1x regression passed with independent window and
launch-channel clients. GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumer focus,
IME-wire and launcher regressions passed on Alpine/WSL. The scale callback test
checks reordered output identity, unchanged scale before `done`, targeted dirty
notification and physical-dimension bounds. The final desktop binary SHA-256 was
`4ea8135ed98a95b9a65dfebf0413c80e37371069a8c3483d4179d598df98f5bb`;
the modified compositor binary was
`33e3205910ae82be7a0635b44ebaed973c0946d26804bc8a16fda9523c4de177`.
Both modified compositor source files, the initial failure, captures and logs
are in `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-output-scale2-final-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`,

## Retire the native input context at the focus edge (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `b586ccfeafacbb8b1a8a8242243d73765330963c`, the private
desktop test host now disables the native input context as soon as a processed
frame moves focus away from its search field. Previously a keyboard batch could
move focus away and back before the outer poll loop noticed, leaving the old
enable interval active. A controlled callback regression performs that
round-trip and checks that a late preedit from the earlier interval does not
reset the newly enabled one. A mutant with the new focus-edge check removed
failed that assertion (exit 1).

The controlled Wayland-server test additionally observes the actual native
disable request before the outer loop and verifies a fresh enable after focus
returns. GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan focus, IME-wire, transaction and
launcher tests passed on Alpine/WSL; independent AT-SPI also passed with the
sanitized host. Fedora passed the focus and wire tests and the combined Aetheri
owner/AT-SPI/Compose recovery scenario. The combined scenario still permits a
lost first Compose key and explicit retry, as documented below; it does not
independently exercise this new keyboard focus edge on the Aetheri compositor.
The desktop binary SHA-256 was
`8443afeb613b79a57de3d69985670f445c65897b3df1298fde02aa109ea56e30`.
Results are at `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-ime-focus-edge-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `2b5e8b429e7fe392383dcb2442d23710b74370ac321ac31a6d9882918b35e50a`.
The mutant's local result is `/tmp/nexa-ime-focus-mutant-result.log` on WSL;
the first shell wrapper accidentally reported success despite the failed test,
so a direct invocation was repeated and returned exit 1. Physical devices,
external input methods and general multi-field hosts remain separate gates.

## Reset a rejected stale Compose draft (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `f0dfad0e199e5c1ac87afcd01a000b3edadbfcda`, the private
desktop test host records the serial that enabled its current native input
context. If a nonempty preedit from that same enable interval is rejected
because the text revision has advanced, it disables that context, retires the
transaction and subsequently enables a fresh one. A reply from an earlier
enable interval does not reset the current context. This prevents a rejected
draft from remaining invisibly active; it does not replay the lost Compose key.

In the owner/AT-SPI test without the test-only roundtrip, the actual Aetheri
Compose provider delivered old serial 2 after serial 3. The desktop rejected
the draft, disabled and re-enabled the provider. Afterward ordinary `'e` stayed
ordinary text rather than completing the rejected composition; after clearing
it, a fresh Compose sequence produced `é`. The test allowed bounded retries
when a second immediate edit itself produced another stale serial. Settings and
Terminal then completed their owner-managed sequence and all processes exited
0. A previous test attempt exposed that second race and is retained.

Static/shared GCC and Clang ASan/UBSan layer focus, IME-wire, transaction and
launcher tests passed on Alpine/WSL. A focus regression checks that an older
enable interval does not reset the new one, and that the same-interval stale
preedit does; the pre-fix test failed at the latter assertion. Independent
AT-SPI with the updated ASan host passed. The combined successful runner was
`652899a7713740043312631eca9a037a8148273294a2b125b52d808ab7619f32` and
desktop binary `8d21e00aa6b458fc57fdfc70bbc6247d5e9cd46463b3733284617065f54a7c43`.
Logs are in `D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-owner-ime-reset-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`,
SHA-256 `38315d2efec55b5aaa1df9aa17487ad8c402f62c1890521123689dac6de34583`.
The first key may still need retry; external input methods, model/field
replacement beyond this search fixture, production owner adoption, physical
devices and human accessibility acceptance remain open.

## Compose serial after an accessibility edit (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `5463c68f8157784d13d086c7ef9cb627355440d0`, an opt-in
`--no-ime-barrier` diagnostic reproduced the combined-owner failure without
waiting for the full timeout. After AT-SPI changed and cleared the query, the
desktop had published text-input serial 3 for the new field revision. The
compositor's subsequent preedit arrived with serial 2 and was rejected as
`NX_ERROR_STATE` (first accepted serial 3). This was observed twice. The trace
records serials, result code and opaque revision only, not input content.

A deterministic transaction regression checks that an old-revision preedit is
discarded, and a fresh preedit and Unicode commit remain admissible. Static and
shared GCC and Clang ASan/UBSan transaction, focus and IME-wire tests passed on
Alpine/WSL. The combined Aetheri owner/AT-SPI/Compose run also passed with its
test-only Wayland barrier. Test logs, including both failing unsynchronized
runs and the barrier pass, are in
`D:/NexaUI/out/nexaui-owner-ime-trace-evidence-20261106.tar.gz`, SHA-256
`cf434cfc4d458cf6584261f3b535a4fde535db5762181e39fe4a968179f66a29`.
The pass used runner SHA-256
`ccb1f091b6da1fdf91d3f1a63d708b3476e6355784b552943ea54e61cf1fdd7c` and
desktop binary `9a281340939a0e41c5b6ec633e6dfe92dd05b24b739b7cb7f7a35d27b783f8b7`.

The old serial is insufficient evidence to safely apply a composition to a new
text revision or field. Therefore this does not relax rejection or claim the
input-loss problem fixed. A reliable unsynchronized handoff needs a reviewed
contract for provider ordering/reset and field identity, including late replies
and focus replacement. The recorded run uses virtual input and pre-existing
Aetheri test binaries; physical and production-provider acceptance remains open.

## Current desktop owner protocol (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `6ec56009c4115082e5bc3aef32a403945f2e22a6`, the private

`A3/E3/X3` replies. A version-2 reply now fails closed. In owner mode Ctrl+Q
sends one `Q30`, blocks later launch requests, and leaves the desktop alive
for the owner's signal after child shutdown. Existing search/window activation
is not blocked. A socketpair regression tests the version, rejected old reply,
duplicate close request and a later ignored launch, using GCC static/shared and
Clang ASan/UBSan builds. The layer focus and IME-wire regressions also passed.

An independent peer on the isolated Fedora Aetheri test compositor exercised
the updated Settings and Terminal launch messages, pending/retry/exit handling,
window observation, input and three virtual docks; all three processes exited
0. Results are in `/tmp/nexaui-shell-map-f7fc-9NHe0df8/layer-run-v3/` on the
carrier; desktop binary SHA-256 is
`eff18402f96792ab9b2b70d0f5b9b84b29005030fffc36c2f0e0c72e48b65bf9`.
This peer test used the previously recorded `c1c85ae` model snapshot and test
compositor, not the original supervisor. The later owner run above provides
separate headless evidence. No Aetheri checkout or pinned SDK was modified.

## Input after output retirement is scheduled (2026-11-06)

test host now rejects input and frame actions as soon as output removal or layer
close is observed; object destruction remains deferred until dispatch returns.

before teardown and checked that neither changed the app-owned query. Before
the fix, the key changed `AB` to `ABx` and the test exited 1. A controlled
Wayland-wire test delivered a pending pointer-sync reply in the same interval
and checked that no window activation resulted. On Alpine-Dev/WSL, static,

tests; an independent AT-SPI client also passed in each profile. The AT-SPI
harness launched its own registry despite a WSLg runtime-directory ownership
warning. These tests simulate removal and do not prove physical hotplug or
integration with the production desktop owner.

reordered outputs, duplicate/missing names, removal and repeat mapping. An
isolated mutant that again selects registry-order objects failed the reordered
assertion at `/tmp/nexaui-shell-map-f7fc-9NHe0df8/mutant.log` on the guest.

client headers >=1.20 and a version-4 output global; this does not change the

static consumer callback, model, launcher and IME wire tests passed, as did the

controlled callback test demonstrates reordered mapping. The original

on the guest. SHA-256: SDK library

and Aetheri source archive `a2577a6c9635c9133c1bc97d916154263cbb8d9f19f2ad975555cde5bc6db0af`.

## Preserving an unrelated AT-SPI action across the guard (2026-11-06)

On base `57d1b74afa92babca0e598bf5b892eff3629af13`, the numeric test host
submits both Apply and an independent `Other action` in the preedit guard
frame and handles the latter's activation in that frame as well as in normal
frames. The independent client queues both native AT-SPI actions against the
old enabled snapshot while the host is stopped. Weston simple-IME preedit and
a pointer press are also queued before it resumes. Both action calls report
accepted; the guarded Apply does not update the value, while `Other action`
executes exactly once through clean close. The client subsequently sees Apply
disabled and its new activation rejected.

GCC 15.2 static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan static Debug passed under
private Weston 14.0.2, a private AT-SPI registry and virtual input, with leak
detection for the sanitizer run. The pointer-only batch regression passed in
static Debug. An isolated negative omitted only `Other action` from the guard
frame; the new check failed because that action was lost. Its log is
`/tmp/nexaui-other-action-negative-20261106/negative.log`. Positive JUnit
evidence is in `/tmp/nexaui-other-action-{static,shared,asan}-close-20261106/`.

This validates two queued button actions in this complete test scene, not an
arbitrary application's event loop. A guard frame may also consume keyboard,
text, clipboard, other accessibility work or application state transitions;
every such operation needs correct processing and lifetime handling. Multiple
preedit snapshots, production Aetheri, other IMEs and human assistive acceptance
remain open. No SDK public API or Aetheri source/pin changed.

## Pending AT-SPI action and IME preedit in one batch (2026-11-06)

On base `816e5a9cb9fd37c2664152bc266512f16ac4bba7`, the visible numeric
test host optionally publishes its semantic tree and publishes the disabled
Apply state in its existing preedit guard frame. A separate libatspi/D-Bus
client discovers the real button by host PID. While the test-owned host is
stopped, Weston simple-IME sends preedit, the client sends `DoAction` and
confirms its bus routing, and a pointer click is queued. The resumed host
observes preedit and pointer press in the same routed frame.

The client receives `true` for the action that was queued against the earlier
enabled snapshot. The host does not apply it: focus, preedit, draft and prior
value remain intact. The client then independently observes Apply disabled and
confirms another action is rejected. After the IME commits invalid text, a
pointer activation leaves the previous numeric value unchanged. GCC 15.2
static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan static Debug passed under private Weston
14.0.2, a private AT-SPI registry and virtual input; leak detection was enabled
for the sanitizer run. The existing pointer-only batch test passed again.

In an isolated copy with only the guard disabled, the same test lost preedit
and executed `APPLY 1 12345`, despite the composition. Its failing log is
`/tmp/nexaui-pending-atspi-negative-20261106/negative.log`; the base archive
SHA-256 is `51b58ced23d2b7084ea30aaff34dd205450ca62c2a38842c1b98cc70f2a5ca8c`.
Positive JUnit evidence is in `/tmp/nexaui-pending-atspi-{static,shared,asan}-final-20261106/`.

The `true` reply means the old snapshot accepted the request, not that the
application completed it. This fixture discards the blocked action; its extra
frame could consume other pending UI work and is not a general host barrier.
Multiple preedit changes per poll, other actions, other IMEs, actual Aetheri,
physical input and human assistive acceptance remain open. No SDK API or
Aetheri source/pin changed.

## Wayland v1 preedit serial and queued pointer input (2026-11-06)

On base `9416e2a5e81e15783ea716c05decc5fef18b2dd8`, Wayland text-input-v1
now accepts an older preedit serial after a caret-only state update if it remains
within the current activation and the advertised editor/surrounding revision is
unchanged. A changed editor, changed or withdrawn snapshot, old activation and
untagged old serial remain rejected. This aligns preedit with the existing
revision-guarded commit path. The controlled v1 server checks the accepted old
reply and changed-revision, changed-target and withdrawn-snapshot negatives;
the v1/v3 tests pass in GCC 15.2 static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan static.
An unmodified-base negative failed the new accepted-preedit assertion at
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-batch-negative-20261106/serial-negative.log`.

The numeric test host also experiments with publishing an IME-preedit-disabled
Apply frame *before* routing the next native event batch. It has no pending UI
events or published AT-SPI tree at that point. A private Weston simple-IME
test stops only its own client, observes the provider's preedit on the protocol,
queues a pointer click, and resumes it. It requires both events in one routed
frame and checks that preedit and focus survive, followed by an invalid commit
that does not update the numeric value. Static/shared/sanitized builds passed;
the sanitized case passed twice. With the serial fix retained but the host guard
disabled in an isolated copy, the same batch lost focus and preedit:
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-batch-negative-20261106/barrier-only-negative.log`.
The base archive SHA-256 is
`a18c60f25d2f2dd85267dd3a23054fc5d8047ba0fc0360ca42db04854250e9ca`.

Earlier attempts using fixed sleeps were intermittent: an ASan run discarded
preedit before the click. Logs are retained in
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-batch-asan-20261106/` and
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-batch-asan-final-20261106/`. The final test waits for
observable provider and surrounding-text protocol state and does not treat a
missing batch as a pass. Separate preedit, pointer and keyboard regressions and
the four Settings simple-IME cases pass in static Debug. The first Settings
attempt had two missing test executables; after building the existing focus
client, all four passed at `/tmp/nexaui-preedit-settings-complete-20261106/`.

This is one host experiment, not a new general event-loop API: an extra frame
can consume queued context work and affect accessibility publication in other
hosts. The window query exposes the latest preedit snapshot, not historical
snapshots for every queued event. Multiple preedit changes in one poll, other
IMEs, AT-SPI actions in the same batch, actual Aetheri, physical input and human
acceptance remain open. No Aetheri pin or worktree was changed.

## Disabled pointer target preserves composition focus (2026-11-06)

On base `ca19ea2b1900876ee2a700f144053f08f67cf586`, primary pointer down on a
disabled region preserves the existing focus while still blocking activation
and underlying controls. Previously the pointer path cleared focus; the touch
path already retained it. Empty-space clicks still clear focus. The behavior and
previous-frame hit-test boundary are described in the [input contract](../concepts/input.md).

The numeric test view disables Apply after observing IME preedit. With private
Weston 14.0.2 simple-IME, a virtual pointer click on that disabled button after
its frame retains focus, apostrophe preedit, the draft and the prior value. The
IME then commits `á`; the resulting invalid draft is not confirmed when Apply
is clicked. Static/shared GCC 15.2 and static Clang 22 ASan/UBSan passed; the
interaction suite also checks disabled overlays, no click-through and the
unchanged empty-space behavior. Keyboard and non-preedit pointer regressions
passed in the static profile; numeric-field and text-pointer unit regressions
passed in shared and sanitized profiles.
The independent large-text/numeric AT-SPI regression also passed in the static
profile, including its disabled-action negative.

An isolated archive of the unmodified base, with only the new test fixture
copied in, failed the same preedit test: `AFTER_CLICK 0 1234 0` rather than
`AFTER_CLICK 1 1234 1`. Its trace is retained at
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-negative-case-20261106/negative.log`; the archive SHA-256
is `53728bbe0827ec065d0b188220596aaae907be9056000ebe3c29456da9a196d6`.
The positive static/shared/sanitized runs are under
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-guard-fixed-20261106/`,
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-fixed-shared-20261106/`, and
`/tmp/nexaui-preedit-fixed-asan-20261106/`.

This proves a previously published disabled frame under one IME, not a change
and pointer press in the same event batch. Cross-platform native input, other
IME providers, accessible action during composition, physical input, human
assistive review and actual Aetheri integration remain open.

## Visible numeric Apply with keyboard and IME input (2026-11-06)

native numeric host has an opt-in 320x120 test view with a real-font `Apply`
button using `nx_numeric_field_commit`. On private Weston 14.0.2/Xvfb, virtual
keyboard input focuses the number, enters `5`, tabs to Apply, confirms 12345,
returns to the field, enters the invalid draft `-`, and verifies a second
activation preserves 12345. GCC 15.2 static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan
static Debug profiles passed; leak detection was enabled for the latter.

A separate composed test starts Weston simple-IME, verifies its v1
`commit_string` for `5`, and uses an actual virtual pointer click on the
visible button to confirm 12345. All three profiles passed. The previous
independent clipboard and numeric preedit regressions also passed in static
Debug. This pointer run does not click during an uncommitted preedit and does
not prove keyboard navigation when that IME consumes Tab or Enter. It uses a
test host rather than an Aetheri production surface; other IMEs, physical input,
full accessibility and human review remain open.

The initial command-stream capture, inspected locally, is
`/tmp/nexaui-numeric-apply-20261106.ppm` (SHA-256
`c86dc9337540c1a94dccb96e40d13bcf31a42632feb1abc8964e07c9b82241f2`),
using DejaVu Sans SHA-256
`7da195a74c55bef988d0d48f9508bd5d849425c1770dba5d7bfc6ce9ed848954`.
It is a headless rendering of the same draw commands, not a physical display
screenshot. Executed evidence is retained under
`/tmp/nexaui-apply-composed-{static,shared,asan}-20261106/` and
`/tmp/nexaui-apply-keyboard-final-20261106/`. No SDK implementation changed.

## Native accessibility action for numeric confirmation (2026-11-06)

On base `73b0a208130794475d9499a7aa99075f091d69c0`, the existing private
Wayland AT-SPI integration fixture now submits an `Apply number` button and
uses the 0.1.20 `nx_numeric_field_commit` API for its action. An independent
libatspi client verifies the native push-button role, changes the numeric draft
through EditableText and activates the button. An invalid `bad` draft leaves
the prior value 1234 unchanged; a valid `42` updates it to 42. The same client
checks that an explicitly disabled button rejects the action before reenabling
it. Host-side action counts and values are checked independently of the client.

GCC 15.2 static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan static Debug tests passed on
Alpine/WSL under private Weston 14.0.2 and a private AT-SPI registry, with leak
detection for the sanitizer run. This semantic fixture does not paint a visible
Apply label or combine the action with a live IME; it does not establish keyboard
access when an IME consumes Tab/Enter, active-composition policy, an Aetheri
app integration or human screenreader and physical-input acceptance. No SDK
implementation or public API changed in this step.

## Explicit numeric confirmation after native input (2026-11-06)

The 0.1.20 development candidate on base
`99f6dc1373aad73ff942b0571f06bbdd7969180f` adds
`nx_numeric_field_commit`: an application action can validate and confirm the
processed draft using the same bounds and existing commit logic as Enter. It
does not replace the draft or undo history, commit active preedit, or interpret
IME text as a command. Invalid and out-of-range drafts preserve the value;
null and non-finite/reversed bounds are rejected. The
[text contract](../concepts/text.md) includes an example.

On Alpine/WSL, GCC 15.2 static/shared and Clang 22 ASan/UBSan static Debug
numeric-field tests passed, including invalid arguments, invalid/out-of-range
drafts and undo/redo after explicit confirmation. With Weston 14.0.2 simple-IME
the same three profiles passed a real virtual `5` text commit, followed by a
test-only signal representing the application action; the confirmed value changed
from 1234 to 12345 before preedit and invalid composition were checked. The
independent clipboard regression passed in the GCC static profile. This does
not demonstrate an accessible Apply control, keyboard access to one, Enter
delivery from this IME, a decision about active composition, other providers,
actual Aetheri integration or human/physical acceptance. Those remain open.
The Wayland-only installed-package probe also built and ran its C17 consumer
against static and shared Release SDKs, including the new public symbol and
the expected Vulkan-component negative; log:
`/tmp/nexaui-commit-package-20261106.log`.

Static/shared/sanitized library SHA-256 respectively:
`9b75e596955b514e15babd37ef1de6d606ae601e07b37e2076e277f26f7c7ae7`,
`71acb70a65da34cd741f7f134defe9ee34fb644932955e3095b319decc8a51ad`,
`a6ca453465f78fb57bc2fd3aad0c3b914ef0502c13482ebcce3b543a4aeb5f0b`.
The candidate is a pre-1.0 SDK change; Aetheri's reviewed pin is unchanged.

## Numeric valid draft and IME confirmation gap (2026-11-06)

On NexaUI base `d799663d28886f567d096dbe4bf27a94c2542ef3`, the expanded
private Weston 14.0.2 simple-IME test sends a real virtual `5` into the numeric
field. The v1 wire trace reports `commit_string` for that digit; the field
reports the valid draft `12345` while its last confirmed value remains `1234`.
The existing apostrophe preedit, invalid `á` commit and observed Ctrl+Z path
then still pass. The independent Wayland clipboard peer regression also passed
in the same GCC 15.2 Alpine/WSL Debug, static Wayland-only build.

A separate stricter experimental run attempted Enter after the valid draft and
waited for confirmed value `12345`. It failed: Weston simple-IME sent a v1
`commit_string` containing a newline and no confirmation was observed. Its
JUnit and complete protocol trace are retained in
`/tmp/nexaui-numeric-valid-run-20261106/simple-ime.xml`; the passing case is
`/tmp/nexaui-numeric-valid-run-20261106-b/simple-ime.xml`. The failed experiment
used the same base and changed only the numeric test host and driver, including
the wider test range. This does not prove that all providers handle Enter this
way, or that a text commit can safely be treated as a command. A reliable
keyboard confirmation path with this provider, other IMEs, actual Aetheri and
human/physical acceptance remain open. No public SDK behavior changed.

## Measured four-button desktop dock layout (2026-11-06)

On candidate base `6ced584aa23aec468bf6ed607228d3b0a5ac2045`, the 320-logical-
pixel dock no longer divides its four buttons into equal 70-pixel widths.
The old layout left 54 pixels of text width after padding, while the existing
14-pixel DejaVu Sans `Settings` layout measured 57.42 pixels. The view now
allocates button widths from actual shaped label widths, preserves at least
44 pixels per hit target, and clips each button's drawing to its own bounds.
If the available width cannot accommodate every label, proportional fallback
keeps those hit targets and clips text rather than overlapping its neighbor.

At width 320, the scene test checks actual semantic button rectangles and
compares the full English and German labels with each button's padded width.
DejaVu Sans, Sans Bold and Sans Mono passed in GCC static/shared and Clang
ASan/UBSan profiles. A test-only equal-width mutant failed because Bold
`Settings` needed 66.08 pixels while its available text width was 62. The
German DejaVu Sans headless 320x84 capture was visually inspected; it is a
local test image, not a product-design approval. The three profiles also
passed layer launcher/focus/IME-wire and independent multi-view AT-SPI checks,
plus private Weston/Xvfb keyboard preview. An intermediate variant keeping
16 pixels of padding failed the Bold German `Suchen` fit; the final layout
uses 4 pixels on each side. The tested Aetheri desktop model is from immutable
commit `7374f381719397abc100dcb784becf59c86d1462`.

This is a bounded example with three fonts, two languages and logical 320x84
geometry. Other fonts, overflow fallback readability, 200% text scale, physical
DPI, real private Aetheri shell and human visual/assistive acceptance remain
open. No SDK implementation or Aetheri checkout changed.

## Files dock intent and owner protocol (2026-11-06)

On candidate base `96e8e06e67e5d95a47758276615c05b660ea30ff`, the desktop
view adds Files/Dateien as the fourth dock action. The view emits the existing
launch intent without mutating the application model; its button is disabled
while Files is pending. The private layer test host uses the Aetheri model's
app-selector functions rather than treating every non-Settings app as Terminal.
A local socketpair verifies `L2F`, pending propagation across three models,
accepted `A2F`, exit `X2F`, failed `E2F`, and rejection of a duplicate click.
A mutant using the old two-app selector sent `L2T` and failed this test.

With an immutable archive of Aetheri `7374f381719397abc100dcb784becf59c86d1462`,
GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds passed the scene, socketpair,
layer-focus and controlled IME-wire regressions. The scene checks keyboard
activation, the German semantic name and in-bounds geometry at 320 logical
pixels. An independent libatspi client invoked the Files dock button, selected
Files and Terminal search results, and exercised search text and activation.
The native accessibility host uses simulated geometry/focus. The first build
attempt asked an existing build tree for the new target before reconfiguring;
the final profiles reconfigured and passed. Older Aetheri snapshots lacking
Files/selector helpers now fail explicitly at configure time.

The socket is a test owner, not the Aetheri supervisor or a launched process.
Actual shell integration, native keyboard/pointer operation on its private
role, pixel-level layout/translation review, physical scaling and human
assistive acceptance remain open. No SDK ABI or Aetheri checkout changed.

## Desktop search native selection owner (2026-11-06)

On candidate base `dcb3f4ddb20222120f1f557131a573b0c996fe10`, the external
desktop view uses the existing single-selection group for search results.
An admitted native selection produces an app-owned `AETHERI_DESKTOP_SELECT`
intent, never a launch. Published selection reflects the confirmed model; the
host must validate/apply the intent and advance its revision. The scene test
checks disabled selection, rejection after a revision change, and unchanged
model/semantics until acknowledgement. Suppressing selection intent generation
in a test-only mutant failed the new assertion.

An independent libatspi client selected a result using the list's Selection
interface, observed the host-confirmed state, then edited the search field and
activated Terminal. GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumers with leak
detection passed using the older Aetheri desktop model snapshot, with blobs
`be57398f32c1e16315510bd95c743559b60c7b52` and
`5a7ed35e92744e5a4b08254b26151efc2025cb34`. A separate GCC static run used
an archive of Aetheri commit `7374f381719397abc100dcb784becf59c86d1462` and
also selected its newer Files search result before Terminal. The independently
changing Aetheri checkout and its uncommitted Settings work were not edited.

The first native test failed its subsequent text edit because the fixture
published the guarded reconciliation frame; it now publishes the following
editable frame. The failed sanitizer client exited early and reported GLib
allocations on its failure path; the final successful sanitizer run had no
reported leak. Native transport uses a private D-Bus registry with simulated
surface geometry/focus, not the production shell or human screenreader input.
The fixed dock does not yet include Files; actual shell ownership, other input
ordering and physical acceptance remain open.

## Files queued focus and navigation ordering (2026-11-06)

On base `4cc5833e84cea2b9d19f0032e3d1947f3d152d6a`, the Files consumer
previously decided an End key using the old focus when Tab and End were
drained in the same native event batch. The private Weston test stopped only
the test consumer, sent both virtual key sequences, and resumed it. Before
the fix, close-to-list Tab followed by End left the selection at item00;
afterwards it selected item19. The reverse Shift-Tab/End sequence preserves
item00 when focus moves from the first row to Close, which still activates.

The consumer now retains one navigation key until the preceding queued UI
events have produced a frame; its next poll is nonblocking. The full native
workflow passed with GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds, with leak
detection enabled, on private Weston/Xvfb. An independent AT-SPI client also
passed selection, filter and button-close checks on the static build. Two
intermediate driver attempts reached the new selection assertion but failed
their subsequent close step because the test no longer had Close focused;
the final driver explicitly verifies the reverse focus transition and closes
via the button. This checks these bounded key sequences, not every possible
native input/IME or accessibility event ordering. The model is a pinned
Aetheri snapshot, input is virtual, and physical and human acceptance remain
open.

## Files navigation respects control focus (2026-11-06)

On candidate base `471348c1b8fee3aa0d75eb129ba1059acc2a1c90`, the
original-model Files consumer sends navigation keys to its list only when a
current row or the tree has focus, or when there is no focused control.
Previously it intercepted those keys on any non-filter control.
The scene regression checks filter, clear, close and selected-row focus;
GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds using the separately installed
SDK and Aetheri snapshot `87ff701f7d793b825c1d9012fc7d4bbd7b30773d` passed.
Restoring the earlier non-filter-only condition in a test mutant failed the
close-control assertion. A private Weston 14.0.2/Xvfb run sent End on clear
and close with virtual keyboard input, checked the unchanged model selection
after each frame, and retained existing list navigation and close behavior.
An initial run used a nonexistent font path; the successful tests used
`/usr/share/fonts/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf`. An initial Weston launch lacked its
private library path and a second lacked the module map; the final run used
both. This is an example consumer with virtual input, not physical input,
the actual Aetheri app or a human assistive-technology acceptance.

## Native accessibility of a virtual 100,000-row tree (2026-11-06)

On candidate base `8632bfbdef912fed11445e5391b148523e709968`, a synthetic
100,000-key application model publishes a virtual tree through the Linux
accessibility host. An independent libatspi process on a private D-Bus and
AT-SPI registry checks the initial visible rows, scrolls to row 50,000,
selects it through its native action, and filters to the even-numbered rows.
At each step the tree exposes at most 21 children. The selected row retains
its name and selected state after filtering, while retained references to a
scrolled-out and a filtered-out row return an error. The host exits cleanly.
GCC static and shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds passed, with leak detection
enabled in the sanitizer run. A test-only change assigning semantic IDs from
row indices instead of stable keys failed the retained-selection assertion.

The host uses simulated geometry and focus, and the scroll and filter requests
are test signals rather than native user input. The model contains synthetic
keys and names, not files; Aetheri's 4,096-entry limit, real application
integration, offscreen navigation, compositor behavior and a human screenreader
task remain open. This evidence does not complete P02, L05 or accessibility
acceptance, and does not change the SDK implementation.

## Named 100,000-row filter workload (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `735031712ce53bb3ca4e41e969679337ff9adccc`, the synthetic
virtual-list workload owns 100,000 named entries and a separate index array.
It scans every name for the substring `report`, rebuilds the 50,000 matching
keys and verifies that the preserved anchor's full semantic name remains
`report-050000.txt`. Visible and overscan rows use the actual model names.
A test-only variant bypassing the filter failed the expected match count.
The workload completed with GCC shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds, including
tracked allocator balance and leak detection.

Three GCC 15.2.0 static Release runs on Alpine/WSL2 x86-64 measured 2.98–3.07 ms
for the 100,000-name scan and 2.58–4.01 ms for the subsequent filtered-index
rebuild. The 1,000 scrolled frame constructions averaged 0.0043–0.0062 ms
per frame. The allocator covering corpus, index and NexaUI objects peaked at
11,883,776 bytes; after warmup its measured scroll loop made no allocations.
The measurements use the C wall clock, are machine/load-specific and have no
agreed target budget. Reversal changes traversal order rather than measuring a
general sorting algorithm.

This still is not a 100,000-file filesystem or Aetheri model: the snapshot's
4,096-entry cap, file I/O, localization, native AT-SPI publication and
presentation remain separate requirements. No SDK implementation changed.

## Synthetic 100,000-row virtual-list workload (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `f9ec0de9f98374077e4a1d1fdec3267ebbcef23d`, the new
`nexaui_virtual_list_bench` builds 100,000 stable-key rows, refreshes and
reverses them, rebuilds a 50,000-row subset while retaining the visible anchor,
and registers only visible/overscan button semantics for 1,000 scroll frames.
It checks the anchor after both changes, limits visits to 32 and checks the
semantic count. A test-only variant using row positions as keys failed the
reorder anchor assertion. GCC shared Debug and Clang ASan/UBSan Debug with
leak detection also executed the workload and balanced the tracked allocator.

On Alpine/WSL2 x86-64, GCC 15.2.0, static Release, three final runs measured
4.84–7.44 ms initial index construction, 4.65–5.89 ms refresh, 4.84–8.42 ms
reorder and 2.03–2.27 ms filtered index rebuild. Mean construction of a scroll
frame ranged 0.0039–0.0048 ms; the final frame had 24 row nodes plus one root.
The instrumented list/context allocator peaked at 7,083,776 bytes and made no
allocations during the measured frames after warmup. Times use the C wall clock;
host load and machine differ, and no accepted performance budget is implied.

The owner callback computes the filtered subset directly; application filter
scanning, file I/O, renderer/presentation, native AT-SPI publication, stalled
clients and actual Aetheri are not measured. The pinned Aetheri Files model
itself still caps a directory snapshot at 4,096 entries. This is not a
100,000-file application or P02 acceptance.

## Large terminal snapshot update events (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `4f8c3426fc56f1d0547aafe8ed5dd2ca37e50025`, the same
application-owned terminal accessible transitions from 96,099-byte Unicode
content to 24,099-byte ASCII and back. An independent libatspi client replays
the bounded UTF-8 text-change events into its own copy, checks all 24,000
cells and 99 separators, and compares each result to a fresh native query.
GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumers with leak detection passed;
each observed three insert and three delete events over both updates. Their
ordinary small-selection AT-SPI regression also passed. A test-only client
that discarded insertion events failed the replay comparison. The host waits
for each update to be observed before the next, and has simulated geometry
and focus. This does not establish throughput, stalled-client behavior,
physical rendering, a human screenreader task or actual Aetheri integration.
No SDK implementation or public contract changed in this candidate.

Read-only inspection of Aetheri `48fcdaf3171370fee355e18c637a970fe0692ad7`
also confirmed that its current development compositor terminates on nested
output loss and reports DRM output loss as a failure. Together with the
initial-only private output snapshot, this leaves live output re-addition an
external session/protocol gate rather than an accepted NexaUI host behavior.

## Large Unicode terminal accessibility snapshot (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `c3fdd31ae945626b40225e2464421bff24b98417`, development
version 0.1.19 introduces `NX_TEXT_SNAPSHOT_MAX_BYTES` (512 KiB) for copied,
application-owned read-only snapshots. The existing 64-KiB limit for writable
text replacement remains unchanged. Both caret and no-caret snapshot variants
accept the new exact bound and reject an oversized length. The separate
terminal consumer requires 0.1.19 and uses that public limit.

A full 240x100 Aetheri model of four-byte Unicode characters failed the
previous 64-KiB frame limit and now renders and publishes 96,099 bytes. An
independent libatspi client read every one of the 24,000 scalars and 99 line
separators. Installed GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumers passed
on Alpine/WSL. Core cell-grid and writable-value tests passed in all three
profiles; exact-bound snapshot tests passed in static and ASan/UBSan builds.
The old-limit terminal test failed at the expected frame assertion, and the
consumer's CMake configuration rejected the earlier 0.1.18 installed SDK. The
external Aetheri snapshot remained unchanged.

The AT-SPI host has simulated geometry/focus and one initial publication; it
does not prove rapid large updates, physical presentation, selected glyph
coverage, human screenreader use, other platform bridges or actual Aetheri
adoption. Bridge snapshot and reply budgets remain separate bounds. This is a
pre-1.0 development version and does not complete T07, A05 or P03.

## Unmapped output fallback in the private test host (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `70a6a91b4f8edfb176f9f6147ab2f63a4ef8f44c`, an output
global announced after the host's initial three is reported as unmapped rather
than aborting the process or assigning it by registry order. It is not bound
and does not recreate a view. A controlled Wayland server announced a real
fourth global through the registry; the host ignored it and the existing IME
wire and pointer scenarios continued. After simulated removal, a second
synthetic announcement remained unmapped; an independent AT-SPI client could
still operate another view. Focus, wire and independent AT-SPI tests passed in
GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan with leak detection on Alpine. An
intentional negative restoring the previous three-output assertion failed on
the actual fourth global; the restored static wire test passed.

Read-only inspection of Aetheri at `48fcdaf3171370fee355e18c637a970fe0692ad7`
found that the development desktop protocol sends output index/name entries
when the private control binds, with no output-add/remove update in that
interface. Its XML Git blob was `d8ae3955153413299071ac0ec20c56870fbf4f75`;
the inspected compositor and desktop files were blobs
`7cf9178f457855a0aa4489a838bf0fa40a022a48` and
`206171d3ccb30f4f552a31fee73d05f2583b1112`. Registry arrival order alone
therefore does not prove a later global's current private output index. Actual
output re-addition, a reliable identity/update contract, native Aetheri
hotplug and human/physical acceptance remain open; no Aetheri files changed.

## Deferred private-host output retirement (2026-11-05)

On candidate base `c3dc49068f54a969edf78473341591fabf4894c6`, the test-only
private layer host no longer aborts on a registry removal. It tracks its three
initial output global names and defers known output or layer-close retirement
until dispatch completes. Retirement cancels pending pointer release and
activation for that output, clears its focus, IME surface and accumulated
pointer deltas, then destroys its accessibility host, presenter, model view,
layer, surface and output proxy. Other views remain available. Unknown removed
globals do not destroy a view. Test-control commands targeting a removed first
output are ignored rather than dereferencing it.

The existing independent libatspi multi-view fixture now drives this same
retirement function: its old German search accessible becomes defunct and the
first view still accepts a query and Terminal intent. Callback tests check
focus removal and cancellation of pointer/activation state. A controlled
Wayland server test uses a real surface and a pending pointer-release sync;
retirement before its reply prevents activation. All three tests passed in
GCC static/shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds with leak detection on Alpine.
A test-only mutant that did not schedule registry removal failed the wire
test's pending-removal assertion; the restored static test passed. The static
SDK was declared at `71b71fa9b7065ec9e49e9afeea69374e8fe0c8ac`; its core
sources are unchanged by this example-only candidate. The borrowed Aetheri
desktop model files have SHA-256 `eead8bbb85ff528b5e42500a288c567d72615de434cf20b33577ab5741ac9e95`
and `fe1bbb4061afa046463ca9190708f93940d4293649baaae3c1eda5960dbd9d10`.

Removal callbacks in these tests are simulated: no Aetheri compositor or real
output was removed. The fixture still requires three initial outputs and
cannot add a replacement output after removal. Native hotplug ordering, full
device-loss behavior, actual Aetheri shell ownership and human/physical
acceptance remain open; this does not complete D05 or W05.

## Labeled nested dialog with independent AT-SPI (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `504943f7be51d9b5d0d4ec869f2ccc4162d04e6f`,
the native nested-dialog fixture shapes and paints its four control labels with
the selected example font, and rejects frames without glyph bitmap commands.
It publishes the same frame's semantics. In a private Weston/D-Bus session,
the original virtual-keyboard flow now runs alongside an independent libatspi
client: the client finds the dialog trigger enabled and focused, observes it
disabled while the first menu action is enabled and focused, and sees the
trigger enabled and focused again after Escape. The action itself remains
keyboard-driven and is verified by the host's counter.

Both keyboard-only and keyboard-plus-AT-SPI cases passed using DejaVu Sans in
GCC static and shared Debug and Clang ASan/UBSan Debug with leak detection
(two cases per profile). An intentional probe expecting the covered trigger to
be enabled failed at the menu state check; the result is
`/tmp/nexaui-nested-accessible-negative-20261105-a/result.json`. The static
tests were rerun after restoring the probe and enabling compiler warnings.
The registered tests require a discoverable example font; the AT-SPI case
additionally requires the libatspi and private D-Bus tools.

These checks establish glyph command emission and native semantic states, not
human inspection of pixels, a screenreader task, physical input, other
compositors or actual Aetheri adoption. No SDK API or implementation changed;
L10 and A02 remain open for their full acceptance.

## Native keyboard for a nested modal and menu (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `9228a2306f46c91bc7eb97dbdfb0593ba8c709ee`,
a visible test-only Wayland software host exercises the existing modal and
popup APIs. On private Weston 14/Xvfb, a virtual keyboard focuses a trigger,
opens the modal and its menu, tabs to a second menu action and activates it.
The first Escape returns focus to the modal trigger; the second returns it to
the original trigger. The host checks the actual focus IDs and action count.

The native driver and existing headless overlay regression passed in GCC static
and shared Debug and Clang ASan/UBSan Debug with leak detection on Alpine/WSL.
An intentional negative control disabling Escape on the inner menu failed at
the expected modal-focus assertion; its original fixture result is
`/tmp/nexaui-nested-keyboard-mutant-20261105-a/result.json`. The host paints
control geometry but does not draw label glyphs or publish native semantics.
Earlier independent AT-SPI/UIA/AX checks cover other selected actions but do
not by themselves prove this exact visible keyboard scenario.

No SDK API or implementation changed. Full rendered labels and visual review,
human screenreader and physical-input acceptance, other compositors and actual
Aetheri integration remain open; L10 is not complete.

## Numeric field with Weston simple-IME (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `b2a29e28823478789693b896f87c9f89b52be988`,
the visible numeric test host opts into the existing borrowed-window IME API.
An independently running Weston 14 `weston-simple-im` supplies apostrophe
preedit and commits `á`; the draft becomes invalid while the committed numeric
value remains 1234. The driver verifies both the model and native v1 wire
events. The host now redraws only when routed events, geometry or a pending
presentation require it; the first continuously redrawing attempt did not
observe preedit and is not counted.

With this provider, Ctrl+Z then produced a native `commit_string` containing
`z`, yielding `1234áz` rather than undoing the draft. This happened in GCC
static and shared Debug and Clang ASan/UBSan Debug with leak detection; the
test explicitly reports that outcome and would also accept a correctly routed
undo. The earlier strict undo run failed and is preserved in
`/tmp/nexaui-numeric-real-ime-20261105-b/simple-ime.xml`. The four prior
Settings simple-IME cases and numeric independent-peer clipboard regression
also passed after the host change. No SDK API or behavior changed.

This is one virtual Weston simple-IME and keyboard map, not evidence that all
IMEs consume the shortcut. Undo with this provider, other installed IMEs,
physical input, human accessibility and actual Aetheri acceptance remain open.

## Numeric clipboard with independent Wayland peers (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `70380daf3e73ffdaa89a5ab34434236ca54d8b24`, a
visible test-only numeric host uses the public software presenter and optional
numeric clipboard callbacks. Under private Weston 14/Xvfb, virtual keyboard
input copies `1234` to an independent `wl-paste` process. Separate `wl-copy`
owners then supply `bad` and `42`: the host observes the invalid draft without
changing its committed 1234, then the valid draft before and after Enter commits
42. Escape performs normal host cleanup. At this candidate the fixture used a
50 ms poll with continuous redraw and was not an idle-performance example.

The registered CTest case executed three consecutive times in GCC static
Debug. The same driver passed once each with GCC shared Debug and Clang
ASan/UBSan Debug with leak detection, on Alpine/WSL; the external peers were
wl-clipboard 2.2.1. A negative control omitting numeric clipboard setup failed
at the independent paste client. An initial complete run timed out on Alt+F4
after exercising the clipboard; the test host now handles Escape explicitly.
Those earlier runs do not count as passing full runs.

These virtual compositor and keyboard checks do not establish real Aetheri
clipboard peers, other compositors, physical input, installed IMEs or human
accessibility acceptance. No SDK API or implementation changed; T05 and I05
remain open for their broader requirements.

## Numeric clipboard selection reversion (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `4ed4db364203c8ca5a82ce25a9621f565c7ded64`,
the controlled Wayland numeric clipboard fixture starts a delayed paste, moves
the selection, restores the original selection, and only then completes the
transfer. The draft and committed number remain unchanged. An intentional
negative control leaving the original selection untouched failed at that same
assertion, confirming the peer's bytes would otherwise be applied. The normal
and numeric asynchronous clipboard tests passed in static GCC, shared GCC and
Clang ASan/UBSan with leak detection (two cases per profile). The widget guide
also now describes the existing optional numeric IME and clipboard APIs rather
than claiming numeric clipboard is unavailable. No SDK implementation changed.

The peer is a controlled Wayland server, not an independent desktop clipboard
client. Actual Aetheri peers, installed IMEs, physical input and human assistive
acceptance remain open; this result does not complete T05 or I05.

## Full Settings example: small-window text reflow (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `71b71fa9b7065ec9e49e9afeea69374e8fe0c8ac`,
the full Settings example shares the existing example-only grapheme wrapper
with the original-model consumer. Long German labels at 128x150 retain their
requested font sizes rather than shrinking to fit; measured title, control and
status heights determine the scrollable layout. The public SDK is unchanged.
The copied installed example now includes the shared helper explicitly.

The scene regression passed with DejaVu Sans, Serif and Sans Mono. The scene
and three controlled text-input-v3 tests passed in static GCC, shared GCC and
Clang ASan/UBSan builds. A private Weston virtual keyboard exercised focus,
status paging, apply and clean close at the minimum size. The previous native
diagnostic used the former fixed 70-pixel viewport top and initially failed;

An independently copied software consumer built against the installed static
SDK declared at the base revision passed native keyboard and independent
AT-SPI apply/retry. Four private Weston simple-IME cases passed, including
composition, both focus cancellations and selection replacement. A preliminary
IME run lacked a built focus client and two cases failed; it is not counted.

These are automated virtual fixtures, not human screenreader, physical input,
200% text-scale, other fonts/scripts or actual Aetheri integration acceptance.
The installed SDK contains unchanged core sources from the stated base; the
example changes were the working candidate, not that committed revision.

## Bounded ASCII emergency wrapping (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `c075b03a8405c11898cc7cbb081c61260e768884`, the
Settings example's emergency wrapper can derive breaks for one unbroken
printable ASCII run from its existing shaped caret positions. It requires one
line, an ordered caret per byte and monotonic finite positions. The result is
reshaped and its maximum line width checked; failures and all other text use
the prior grapheme-by-grapheme path. Accessibility names remain the original
bytes. The helper is internal to the example; no SDK API or dependency changed.

The new direct test checks exact byte preservation, grapheme-boundary breaks,
measured width and an impossible-width failure over ASCII, ligature candidates,
spaces, combining sequences and Hebrew. DejaVu Sans, Serif and Sans Mono passed
on static, shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumer builds (nine runs), with one
full Settings scene regression per build. A deliberately corrupted ASCII byte
copy failed the direct test at the expected byte comparison. An initial test
incorrectly assumed that every long string required inserted hard breaks;
the combining input could already wrap naturally, so that assertion was corrected.
The private Sway virtual-pointer and AT-SPI resize workflows passed on all
three builds (six runs). These are automated fixtures, not human reading or
physical input.

The same optional 16-display benchmark, DejaVu Sans, 120-byte unbroken ASCII
names, German 200% and 128x150, was run three times per source on Alpine/WSL2
with GCC 15.2.0, a Release consumer and the same installed static Debug SDK.
The exact base's cold frame took 294–337 ms and subsequent frames 18–27 ms;
the candidate's cold frame took 133–153 ms and subsequent frames 18–21 ms.
This wall-clock frame-construction experiment excludes native presentation;
host load, other text and other machines may differ. The selected display's
name includes a suffix and takes the general path. No target performance
budget, complex-script improvement, real Aetheri adoption or physical
responsiveness is established.

## Cached original-model Settings text layouts (2026-11-05)

The consumer candidate based on `d74583a03cb497b21cb616a421b3d848f8e8fd05`
retains at most 15 label and 16 display layouts, keyed by original text,
logical width, text size and small-layout mode. Unused display slots are
released; new layouts are created before their old slot is destroyed. Tests
exercise the same display ID with changed content, width and scale, alongside
the existing locale, selection, click, clipping and long-grapheme cases. A
mutant that ignored the width key failed the new displayed-height assertion.
Static, shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumers passed those tests with DejaVu
Sans, Serif and Sans Mono (nine runs). The private Sway virtual-pointer and
independent AT-SPI resize workflows also passed for all three builds (six runs).

The optional `aetheri_model_view_benchmark` used 16 synthetic display names of
120 unbroken ASCII bytes, German 200% text and a 128x150 logical frame. On
Alpine/WSL2, GCC 15.2.0, a Release consumer and the same installed static Debug
SDK, three separate baseline runs from the base and three candidate runs each
measured one cold and five repeated frame constructions. Baseline cold ranged
339–427 ms and repeated frames 293–505 ms; candidate cold ranged 352–390 ms
and repeated frames 20–33 ms. These wall-clock observations include layout,
semantic and command construction, not raster presentation; host load varied.
The synthetic worst case is not an approved target or proof of physical-device
responsiveness. Cold layout, other fonts/targets, memory budgets, native input
latency and actual Aetheri adoption remain open.

## Pointer interaction on small Settings labels (2026-11-05)

The consumer candidate based on `5114c345c7833b303087b7d3754b0e63f5c23b5f`
registers clipped, non-focusable hit regions for the separately painted radio
and display labels at small sizes. Pointer presses transfer focus to the
existing control, and label clicks update its original model. Radios are built
after label input is resolved so checked semantics and marks agree in the same
frame; stale display labels are disabled. The ordinary controls and tab order
are retained, and no new accessibility nodes are added.

Controlled tests select labels in both directions and check same-frame checked
states, focus, cancellation when released outside, a display becoming stale
after input is queued, and no later replay. These scene tests passed with three
DejaVu fonts in static, shared and Clang ASan/UBSan consumers (nine runs).
On private Sway at 128x150, a virtual pointer clicked the Light label, after
which the existing keyboard resize/close flow succeeded for all three builds.
An old `5114c345c7833b303087b7d3754b0e63f5c23b5f` view, with only the focused
rectangle diagnostic backported to its host, failed the same native check:
the Wayland trace shows pointer enter at (24,130), button press and release,
and the original model remained unchanged. A separate AT-SPI resize regression
passed with the candidate's static consumer. Real physical input, native display
label clicking, screenreader review and actual Aetheri acceptance remain open.

The first virtual-pointer setup used three short-lived wlrctl devices; the
target saw pointer enter but the click device disappeared before the target
bound it, so those failures do not count as product evidence. The passing test
uses one guarded test helper retaining its device across motion and click;
the `wlr-protocols` 0_git20250702-r0 package was privately extracted and its
SHA-256 was `6017705dfd18698ea4b24c1adce8541747eb002f59307758b752f3804a2f205f`.

## Original-model Settings at 128x150 (2026-11-05)

The consumer candidate based on `e71ee304f1e6c23fa562d9e4b11f7e8e12152079`
uses a fully scrollable small layout below 320x240. Radio marks and display
buttons have separate wrapped labels; Page Up/Down can reveal labels taller
than the viewport. When an unbreakable word exceeds the available width, the
example inserts visible line breaks at existing grapheme boundaries, retaining
the original bytes in the semantic name. The minimum rendered size is now
128x150; smaller windows still do not receive a new frame. A selected mark's
click target is its row, not the separately drawn text below it.

Controlled scene tests cover German at 200% through 128/160/240/319x150,
keyboard focus visibility, paging, semantic selection of a display, and an
unbroken display name containing 40 combining-character graphemes whose
accessible name retains the original bytes. Static, shared and Clang
ASan/UBSan consumers each passed with DejaVu Sans, Serif and Sans Mono (nine
runs). Private Sway virtual-keyboard and independent AT-SPI resize workflows
passed at 128x150 for each of those three builds (six runs). The pre-change
`e71ee304f1e6c23fa562d9e4b11f7e8e12152079` host with the same installed SDK
timed out waiting for its 128x150 frame. A 128x150 high-contrast headless
image was inspected; it showed partial long text before the emergency wrap
and the split label afterwards. Human readability, other fonts/IME/layouts,
physical input, the cost of many long labels, and Aetheri adoption remain open.

## Virtual keyboard across a native Settings resize (2026-11-05)

On the consumer based on `7ab4c7fc4fb83801a38e67e1adade1695766d847`, a
private headless Sway/Pixman test used wtype 0.4-r0 to send virtual-keyboard
events. At 320x240 and default 100% English text, Tab moved focus through the
first two radio controls and Space changed the original model to Light. After
restoring 800x480, the diagnostic frame retained focus on Light; Tab and Space
selected High contrast, and Escape closed the process successfully. Static,
shared and Clang ASan/UBSan installed consumers all passed (three runs,
exit 0). The existing AT-SPI resize mode also passed again with the shared
consumer. An old host/view from `84ec8384ef30f36f3c2e2945e46e88d52ea46d63`
with the frame diagnostic added failed before keyboard input because it never
submitted a 320x240 frame; this does not isolate keyboard handling.

The first keyboard attempt without an explicit input delay reached and changed
Light but timed out on the first Tab after restore; its log showed focus still
on Light. The cause was not established. The passing driver invokes wtype with
a 100 ms sleep before each key and waits for observed frames/state rather than
extending test deadlines. The wtype Alpine package was extracted privately, not
installed system-wide; its package SHA-256 is
`e3237925ad960fd8e8aaa38bc9b0eb3033c1802a6e62be5a040747b13d02833e`.
This does not establish physical keyboard, other layouts, active IME, real
screenreader, physical monitor or actual Aetheri acceptance.

## AT-SPI actions across a native Settings resize (2026-11-05)

On the consumer and SDK based on `6c7253db0186e59e938552460a3fad42acd08c7c`,
the independent libatspi client selects high contrast, German and 200% text,
then pauses while private headless Sway resizes the actual software-rendered
window from 800x480 to 320x240. It observes the semantic window's new size,
checks the previous selections and changes text size to 150%. After restore to
800x480 it observes that size and the retained selections, chooses the second
synthetic display and closes via AT-SPI. The host's submitted frame/model logs
and Sway geometry are checked independently. Installed static/shared and
Clang ASan/UBSan consumers passed on Alpine/WSL2 with private Sway/Pixman and
a private accessibility bus (three runs, exit 0).

As a negative control, the same test and SDK used the original Settings view
and host from `84ec8384ef30f36f3c2e2945e46e88d52ea46d63`, with only the
current frame-size diagnostic added to the old host. Sway reported 320x240,
but the old host did not submit the required small frame; the client timed out
waiting for the resize handoff and exited 1. This exercises the old 480x380
cutoff, not the separately tested same-size configure wake. Native keyboard
interaction during resize, real input methods, human screenreader review,
physical displays and actual Aetheri adoption remain open.

## Same-size Wayland configure and Settings resize (2026-11-05)

On the candidate based on `993e02f1e352b3e38df855c43070ea70b7f55e2f`, an
unchanged-size Wayland configure queues `NX_EVENT_WAKE` while a visible window
has an external renderer. This lets an event-driven owner submit the surface
commit associated with the configure. Size-changing configures already emit a
resize event; the adapter's own background still commits without this wake.
The controlled protocol test failed on the base at `wakes>0`, then passed with
the candidate, and checks that releasing the renderer removes the extra wake.
Eight fractional-protocol cases plus that management test passed 9/9 in each
static, shared and Clang ASan/UBSan Wayland-only configuration. Initial broader
CTest selections included unbuilt binaries and are not counted as passes.

An installed C17 original-model consumer in each of those three configurations
ran under private headless Sway 1.12-rc3/Pixman as an unprivileged user. The
fixture waited for the tiled and floating frames, resized to 320x240, restored
800x480, checked each submitted frame and unchanged default model/display
fixture, then closed via SIGTERM (exit 0). Earlier attempts issued resize while
the floating transition was still settling and timed out; the sequenced fixture
passes on both old and new SDKs, so it does not establish that the wake fix
caused the resize result. It also does not test keyboard, AT-SPI or a real
Aetheri session during resize. Physical/human acceptance, other compositors,
Vulkan-specific presentation and windows below 320x240 remain open.

## Original-model Settings narrow view (2026-11-05)

On the consumer tree based on NexaUI
`84ec8384ef30f36f3c2e2945e46e88d52ea46d63`, the installed C17 example now
wraps radio labels and sizes their rows for the actual measured text. At narrow
widths the title is part of the scrollable content, leaving room to reveal
focused controls. The former host skipped new frames below 480x380; the new
limit is 320x240. Sizes below the new limit still do not receive a new frame.

The controlled scene test covers focus traversal and full focused-control
visibility inside the scroll viewport at widths 320, 360, 399, 400, 440 and
480 with height 240, German and 200% text. Installed static, shared and
Clang ASan/UBSan SDK consumers each passed with DejaVu Sans, Serif and Sans
Mono (nine runs, exit 0). The pre-change scene rejected 320x240. A headless
320x240 frame was visually inspected; an initial frame exposed text/mark
overlap, and the corrected frame was inspected again. This is not a native
resize, hardware scaling, full contrast or human accessibility acceptance.
The existing independent AT-SPI client also passed on private Weston at the
host's normal initial size, changing the appearance, language, text size and
fixture display before closing. That regression does not test a native resize.
The external Aetheri model snapshot's model.c/model.h SHA-256 values are
`3ee6f265faa83b884d387b363e77ac327a69834b2d1aac250ac557a5007b437c` and
`776de312e1cfed97e972c09fb8a56cca0b94748d4e410e5b8278b0335a12e793`,
matching the unmodified model files in Aetheri revision
`6891615dc9803e3a0d328534f17731a130645d4b`. No Aetheri files or pins changed.

## Native two-axis scrollbar actions (2026-11-05)

The added C17 test host and independent libatspi client, on the source tree
based on `93384d478c27b54a1940023546c1f686035f7ef6`, exercise horizontal and
vertical scrollbar increment and decrement actions in one area. The client
observes 0/0, 10/0, 10/10, 0/10 and 0/0 percent before closing the host via
AT-SPI; the host reports its final application-owned offsets. On Alpine/WSL2,
private Weston 14/Xvfb and an unprivileged test process, direct fixture runs
passed for static, shared and Clang ASan/UBSan builds (exit 0 each); the
registered CTest also passed 1/1 in the static build. An older locally installed
static SDK failed the same client at the first horizontal step, observing 0/0
instead of 10/0. The initial CTest attempt failed before running the test
because its existing log directory was not writable by the unprivileged user;
the later run used a writable test-log directory. This is automated private
compositor evidence, not physical input, a human screenreader, other platforms
or Aetheri adoption. The fixture is `nexaui_atspi_scroll_wayland_private` and
requires the existing private Weston wrapper and AT-SPI test dependencies.

## Borrowed host scale and presenter ownership (2026-11-05)

Test source `920787e36bfa298e734be5fc644cfb7cd20e0d2d` exercises the existing
borrowed Wayland presenter with a controlled private role while three buffers
are held: 40x20 at 1x, 80x40 at 2x, then 40x20 at 1x. Server-side assertions
check dimensions, transparent and translucent pixels and scaling of a drawn
rectangle before the held buffers are released. On Alpine/WSL2, Clang 22.1.3,
static Wayland-only Debug with ASan/UBSan and leak detection, this test passed
five consecutive runs. This is a protocol fixture, not real output hotplug,
physical scaling or Aetheri's private role.

The archived Aetheri desktop source `d621c9bba1df8ffd1083999300bcff742022b9dc`
creates SDL custom-role Wayland windows, obtains their surfaces and display,
assigns layer-shell roles and then creates SDL GLES renderers for those windows.
NexaUI's borrowed presenter requires sole ownership of buffer presentation on
its surface. A host integration must explicitly retire or replace the SDL
presenter for each adopted surface; attaching both renderers concurrently is
not established by this test. Role, input, scale, app lifecycle and semantic
ownership remain host obligations. No Aetheri checkout or consumer pin changed.
